===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p1_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p1/benchmark/final_report.md =====
# Final Report

## Question

Investigate ORCHVATE as a company in the United Kingdom. Identify the official website, public social profiles, and whether a UK legal entity is verified. Cite evidence and state what remains unverified.

## Executive Summary

The single most important finding is that Orchvate maintains an official website (https://www.orchvate.com/) and public company profiles on LinkedIn and Facebook that consistently present the organisation as an India‑headquartered social enterprise focused on neurodiversity; LinkedIn lists Bengaluru as headquarters and a 90% neurodivergent workforce. Overall confidence is Medium: the online presence and India HQ are well supported by primary sources, but the absence of evidence for a UK legal entity in the fetched sources is not proof of nonexistence. The key open uncertainty is whether Orchvate is separately registered or operates in the UK (no Companies House or other UK registry record was found in the provided sources).

## Official online presence and organisational details

Orchvate's official website is https://www.orchvate.com/, which self‑identifies as "a social enterprise building and running a robust ecosystem for neurodivergents (NDs) to flourish" and lists services such as ND awareness programs, paid internships, and data‑annotation training [1]. The site provides leadership contact links (e.g., Director Panchali Banerjee) and detailed claims about neurodiversity prevalence and workforce goals, supporting the conclusion that the website is the organisation's primary official web presence [1].

Orchvate also maintains active public social profiles that corroborate the website's self‑description. The LinkedIn company page labels the organisation 'Orchvate', shows Headquarters as Bengaluru, Karnataka, lists "Founded 2019," reports a company size of "11–50 employees," claims a "90% neurodivergent workforce," and links to the orchvate.com website (2,969 followers displayed) [3]. The Facebook page 'Orchvate' likewise emphasizes an India focus, shows contact details including a +91 phone number and info@orchvate.com, and links to the same website (365 followers shown) [2]. Together these three primary sources present a consistent organisational identity and contact points.

**Key Statistics**

- Website: Jargon and services listed on https://www.orchvate.com/ (official site) [1]
- LinkedIn: 90% neurodivergent workforce; Founded 2019; 2,969 followers; HQ Bengaluru, Karnataka [3]
- Facebook: 365 followers; contact phone +91 81053 97506; email info@orchvate.com [2]

**Claims**

| # | Confidence | Source Agreement | Claim |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | High | Strong agreement | Orchvate's official website is https://www.orchvate.com/ and it presents the organisation as a neurodiversity‑focused social enterprise. |
| 2 | High | Strong agreement | Orchvate's LinkedIn company profile lists Bengaluru, Karnataka as headquarters, states 'Founded 2019', reports a 90% neurodivergent workforce, and links to orchvate.com. |
| 3 | High | Strong agreement | Orchvate's Facebook page is an active public profile pointing to an India focus and provides contact details (phone +91 and info@orchvate.com). |

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

**Supporting Evidence**

- Website header/footer and content explicitly show the URL https://www.orchvate.com/ and the self‑description "We are a social enterprise building and running a robust ecosystem for neurodivergents (NDs) to flourish." [1]
- Website lists services (ND awareness, internships, data annotation trainings) and leadership links (e.g., Director Panchali Banerjee) consistent with an operational organisation. [1]

### Claim 2: Orchvate's LinkedIn company profile lists Bengaluru, Karnataka as headquarters, …

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

**Supporting Evidence**

- LinkedIn profile shows Headquarters: Bengaluru, Founded 2019, company size 11–50, and the text "With a 90% neurodivergent workforce..." and links to http://www.orchvate.com. [3]
- LinkedIn follower metric shown as 2,969 followers on the profile header. [3]

### Claim 3: Orchvate's Facebook page is an active public profile pointing to an India focus …

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

**Supporting Evidence**

- Facebook page 'Orchvate' displays intro text 'Orchvate offers innovative approaches to build an ND inclusive society in India', shows contact phone +91 81053 97506 and email info@orchvate.com, and links to orchvate.com. [2]
- Facebook shows follower counts (365 followers) and event/activity posts consistent with the website. [2]

## UK legal registration and verifiable UK presence

None of the fetched sources (official website, LinkedIn, Facebook) asserts a UK legal registration, UK headquarters, or a UK office. The website and social profiles consistently present Orchvate as India‑headquartered (LinkedIn explicitly lists Bengaluru) and display India contact details (Facebook phone number with +91 country code), which together provide no evidence of a Companies House entry or other UK registration within the reviewed materials [1][3][2].

Because the dataset consists solely of the firm's website and its social profiles—primary, self‑reported sources—absence of a UK registration in those sources is informative but not conclusive. The available evidence therefore leaves unverified whether Orchvate has any UK‑registered legal entity (e.g., "Orchvate Ltd"), a UK office, or UK tax/VAT registrations; confirming those requires targeted searches of UK government registries (Companies House, VAT records) or direct confirmation from the organisation. The provided sources do not show such records [1][3][2].

**Key Statistics**

- LinkedIn HQ: Bengaluru, Karnataka (not UK) [3]
- Facebook contact: +91 81053 97506 (India country code) [2]
- No Companies House or UK registration data present in the fetched sources (absence noted) [1][3][2]

| 1 | Medium | Insufficient evidence | There is no evidence in the fetched sources that Orchvate is a UK‑registered legal entity. |
| 2 | Medium | Insufficient evidence | There is no evidence in the fetched sources that Orchvate operates a UK office or subsidiary. |

### Claim 1: There is no evidence in the fetched sources that Orchvate is a UK‑registered leg…

- **Confidence**: Medium
- **Source Agreement**: Insufficient evidence

**Supporting Evidence**

- None of the three fetched sources (orchvate.com, LinkedIn profile, Facebook page) includes or references a Companies House entry, UK registration number, UK address, or UK VAT number. [1][3][2]
- LinkedIn lists Bengaluru, Karnataka as Headquarters and Facebook shows an India phone number (+91), supporting the interpretation that the organisation is India‑based in the available materials. [3][2]

### Claim 2: There is no evidence in the fetched sources that Orchvate operates a UK office o…

- **Confidence**: Medium
- **Source Agreement**: Insufficient evidence

**Supporting Evidence**

- No UK address, UK‑based phone number, or UK office location appears on the website, LinkedIn, or Facebook pages provided. [1][3][2]
- Social profiles and website consistently reference India as the operating region (Bengaluru HQ and India‑focused mission statements). [3][2][1]

## Recommendations

- Who: Researcher or compliance officer. What: Search the UK Companies House register for variations of the name ("Orchvate", "Orchvate Ltd", "Orchvate UK", alternative spellings) and check for active/dissolved filings. Why: Companies House is the canonical source of UK company registrations; the current evidence base (website and social pages) contains no Companies House references, so this search will directly confirm or refute UK registration.
- Who: Researcher or IT investigator. What: Perform a WHOIS lookup for orchvate.com and examine domain registrant details, registration country, registrar, and historical WHOIS records. Why: WHOIS may indicate the country of registration or administrative contact which can help corroborate a UK presence or show India registration details that align with the other sources.
- Who: Procurement/compliance team or legal counsel. What: Request written confirmation from Orchvate via the listed contact (info@orchvate.com) asking for any UK company registration numbers, UK addresses, VAT registration, or proof of a UK subsidiary; request scanned/official documentation if claimed. Why: Direct confirmation from the organisation is the fastest way to verify a claimed UK legal presence; primary sources so far are self‑descriptive and do not show UK registration.
- Who: Researcher. What: Search UK public business directories, HMRC VAT registers, and UK government supplier lists for "Orchvate" and likely variants. Why: Some organisations operate in the UK under slightly different trading names or registered entities; these registries can reveal UK trading relationships even if the corporate name differs.
- Who: Legal/compliance team. What: If commercial relationship or contractual commitments with Orchvate in the UK are planned, engage a UK solicitor to perform formal due diligence (including Companies House filing extracts, beneficial‑ownership checks, and verification of VAT registration). Why: For legal or procurement exposure in the UK, formal legal verification reduces regulatory and contractual risk beyond what web searches can provide.

## Methodology & Confidence

### Methodology

I reviewed three primary organisational sources produced by the company itself and its social presences: the official website (orchvate.com) and company pages on LinkedIn and Facebook. These three sources were the only fetched documents used in this report. Retrieval metadata: Facebook page retrieval timestamp recorded (2026-10-09); LinkedIn and website fetches were included in the dataset without specific retrieval timestamps. All sources are primary/self‑reported (website and social pages); no third‑party regulatory or UK government registrar records were included in the dataset. Notable gaps: no Companies House or HMRC/VAT records were fetched, no UK business directory or third‑party media coverage was provided, and WHOIS/domain registration details were not reviewed in the provided sources.

### Overall Confidence

Conclusions about Orchvate's official website and public social profiles (existence of orchvate.com, LinkedIn profile listing Bengaluru HQ and 90% neurodivergent workforce, and a Facebook page with India contact details) are well supported (High confidence) because multiple independent primary sources present consistent information [1][3][2]. Conclusions about UK legal registration or a UK presence are tentative (Medium confidence) because the available evidence is an absence of UK indicators within a small set of self‑reported sources; absence in these sources does not prove that a UK legal entity does not exist. What would change the assessment: finding a Companies House entry, a UK address or VAT registration, or receiving documentary confirmation from Orchvate showing a UK entity would convert the UK‑registration conclusion to High confidence. Conversely, a definitive statement from Companies House or Orchvate confirming there is no UK entity would support a confident conclusion of nonexistence.

## Open Questions

- Is Orchvate registered as a legal entity in the United Kingdom (Companies House entry for 'Orchvate', 'Orchvate Ltd', or variants)?
- Does Orchvate operate a UK office, hold a UK VAT registration, or maintain a UK‑registered subsidiary?
- Are there additional public UK listings (e.g., HMRC, government supplier registries, business directories, or media reports) for Orchvate that were not included in the fetched sources?

## Report Notes

- Claims are separated from evidence so reviewers can inspect support independently.
- Confidence reflects strength and completeness of available evidence.
- Source Agreement reflects whether cited sources align, conflict, or remain too sparse.

# Researcher Memo

## Question

Investigate ORCHVATE as a company in the United Kingdom. Identify the official website, public social profiles, and whether a UK legal entity is verified. Cite evidence and state what remains unverified.

## Task Mode

standard

## Search Plan

- ORCHVATE company UK official website social media ORCHVATE LinkedIn Twitter Facebook
- site:find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk ORCHVATE ORCHVATE Ltd Companies House

## Summary

Identified an official company website (https://www.orchvate.com/) and public social profiles on LinkedIn and Facebook that present consistent organization details. Evidence from LinkedIn and the website indicates Orchvate is an India-headquartered social enterprise (Bengaluru) founded in 2019, focused on neurodiversity and data-annotation services; LinkedIn states a 90% neurodivergent workforce. I did not find evidence (in the fetched sources) that establishes a UK-registered legal entity for 'Orchvate' (no Companies House record was fetched). Remaining unverified: whether there is any UK legal entity (Companies House registration), and whether Orchvate operates a UK office or UK-registered subsidiary. Recommended next steps: search Companies House and UK business registries for "Orchvate", "Orchvate Ltd", or variant spellings; check domain WHOIS and any UK social/business listings (e.g., Companies House, UK gov supplier lists).

## Key Statistics

- one out of seven people is neurodiverse, per Fetched content from https://www.orchvate.com/
- 197 million people in the country are neurodiverse, per Fetched content from https://www.orchvate.com/
- 10% are dyslexic, per Fetched content from https://www.orchvate.com/
- 5% are dyspraxic, per Fetched content from https://www.orchvate.com/
- 4% have ADHD, per Fetched content from https://www.orchvate.com/
- 1-2% are autistic, per Fetched content from https://www.orchvate.com/

- The unemployment rate for this group is as high as 80%!, per Fetched content from https://www.orchvate.com/
- 365 followers • 17 following, per Fetched content from https://www.facebook.com/orchvate/

## Source Quality Notes

All three sources are primary/organizational: the company's official website (orchvate.com) and its social-media company pages (LinkedIn, Facebook). None are peer-reviewed or third-party regulatory records. LinkedIn provides structured company metadata (headquarters Bengaluru, company size, founded year) which corroborates the website's India focus. There is a recency/coverage gap: no Companies House / UK official registry evidence was fetched, and none of the provided sources asserts a UK registration or UK headquarters. Because the sources are vendor/organizational and social profiles, they reliably show the organization's self-reported details but do not by themselves constitute legal registration proof in the UK.

[32m+[m[32mdef _source_platform(url: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Classify a URL without making ownership or identity claims."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    parsed = urlparse(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m    host = parsed.netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")[m
[32m+[m[32m    path = parsed.path.lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("linkedin.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "linkedin"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("instagram.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "instagram"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("facebook.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "facebook"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host in {"x.com", "twitter.com", "t.co"} or host.endswith("twitter.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "x"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("youtube.com") or host == "youtu.be":[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "youtube"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("tiktok.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "tiktok"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if "companieshouse.gov.uk" in host or ("gov.uk" in host and "company" in path):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "registry"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if "blog" in path or "blog" in host:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "blog"[m
[32m+[m[32m    return "website"[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _source_category(platform: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    if platform in {"linkedin", "instagram", "facebook", "x", "youtube", "tiktok"}:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "social"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if platform == "registry":[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "registry"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if platform == "blog":[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "blog"[m
[32m+[m[32m    return "official" if platform == "website" else "web"[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _access_status(content: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Describe observed public access without attempting authentication."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    text = (content or "").lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m    auth_markers = ("agree & join", "sign in", "log in", "login", "create an account")[m
[32m+[m[32m    if any(marker in text for marker in auth_markers):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "partial_public"[m
[32m+[m[32m    return "fetched"[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _coverage_records(search_results: List[dict], fetched_pages: List[dict]) -> List[dict]:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Create auditable candidate-URL coverage records from observed pipeline data."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    fetched_by_url = {p.get("url"): p for p in fetched_pages if p.get("url")}[m

[32m+[m[32m        page = fetched_by_url.get(url)[m

[32m+[m[32m        candidates.append([m
[32m+[m[32m            {[m

[32m+[m[32m                "source_category": _source_category(platform),[m

[32m+[m[32m                "access_status": _access_status(page.get("content", "")) if page else "not_attempted",[m

[32m+[m[32m            }[m
[32m+[m[32m        )[m

[32m+[m[32m            continue[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _enrich_memo_sources(memo_dict: dict, state: ResearchState) -> dict:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Attach pipeline-observed provenance to LLM-selected sources; never infer ownership."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    coverage = {item["url"]: item for item in state.get("source_coverage", []) if item.get("url")}[m
[32m+[m[32m    fetched = {item.get("url"): item for item in state.get("fetched_pages", []) if item.get("url")}[m
[32m+[m[32m    for source in memo_dict.get("sources", []):[m
[32m+[m[32m        url = source.get("url", "")[m
[32m+[m[32m        observed = coverage.get(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m        if observed:[m
[32m+[m[32m            source.update({k: v for k, v in observed.items() if v is not None})[m
[32m+[m[32m        else:[m
[32m+[m[32m            platform = _source_platform(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m            source.update([m
[32m+[m[32m                {[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "platform": platform,[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "source_category": _source_category(platform),[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "access_status": _access_status(fetched[url].get("content", "")) if url in fetched else "not_attempted",[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "discovery_method": "unknown",[m
[32m+[m[32m                }[m
[32m+[m[32m            )[m
[32m+[m[32m        source.setdefault("identity_status", "unverified")[m
[32m+[m[32m        if url in fetched and fetched[url].get("retrieved_at"):[m
[32m+[m[32m            source["retrieved_at"] = fetched[url]["retrieved_at"][m
[32m+[m[32m    return memo_dict[m

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p1_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p1/patch/test_source_provenance.py =====
from src.graph.nodes import (
    _access_status,
    _coverage_records,
    _enrich_memo_sources,
    _source_category,
    _source_platform,
)


def test_source_platform_classification():
    assert _source_platform("https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/") == "linkedin"
    assert _source_platform("https://instagram.com/orchvate") == "instagram"
    assert _source_platform("https://www.facebook.com/orchvate/") == "facebook"
    assert _source_platform("https://x.com/orchvate") == "x"
    assert _source_platform("https://www.youtube.com/@orchvate") == "youtube"
    assert _source_platform("https://www.tiktok.com/@orchvate") == "tiktok"
    assert _source_platform("https://find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/123") == "registry"
    assert _source_platform("https://example.com/blog.html") == "blog"
    assert _source_category("linkedin") == "social"
    assert _source_category("registry") == "registry"


def test_access_status_is_observational_and_does_not_authenticate():
    assert _access_status("Public company description") == "fetched"
    assert _access_status("Agree & Join\nSign in to see more") == "partial_public"


def test_coverage_records_distinguish_fetched_and_not_attempted():
    search_results = [
        {"url": "https://example.com", "title": "Example"},
        {"url": "https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/", "title": "Orchvate"},
    ]
    fetched_pages = [
        {"url": "https://example.com", "title": "Example", "content": "OK", "retrieved_at": "now"},
    ]
    records = {row["url"]: row for row in _coverage_records(search_results, fetched_pages)}
    assert records["https://example.com"]["access_status"] == "fetched"
    assert records["https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/"]["access_status"] == "not_attempted"


def test_memo_sources_receive_pipeline_provenance_without_identity_inference():
    memo = {
        "sources": [
            {"title": "Orchvate", "url": "https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/", "evidence": ["name"]}
        ]
    }
    state = {
        "source_coverage": [
            {
                "url": "https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/",
                "platform": "linkedin",
                "source_category": "social",
                "discovery_method": "search_result",
                "access_status": "partial_public",
                "retrieved_at": "now",
            }
        ],
        "fetched_pages": [],
    }
    enriched = _enrich_memo_sources(memo, state)
    source = enriched["sources"][0]
    assert source["platform"] == "linkedin"
    assert source["access_status"] == "partial_public"
    assert source["identity_status"] == "unverified"

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p1_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p1/reports/orchvate_p1_implementation_report.md =====
# Company Intelligence Agent — P1 Implementation Report

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Baseline revision:** `604a79a`

**Phase:** P1 — reliable research and evidence

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE, target jurisdiction United Kingdom

## Scope implemented

The first bounded P1 slice adds provenance and coverage metadata without introducing a database, new external provider, authentication, or platform-access bypass.

### Evidence schema

`src/schemas/research.py` → `ResearchSource` now supports backward-compatible fields for:

- `platform` — website, blog, LinkedIn, Instagram, Facebook, X, YouTube, TikTok, registry, or other.
- `source_category` — official, social, blog, registry, or web.
- `discovery_method` — search result, follow-up search, official-site link, direct registry query, or unknown.
- `access_status` — fetched, partial public, login required, blocked, timeout, not attempted, or unknown.
- `identity_status` — unverified, candidate, corroborated, conflicting, or rejected.
- `publication_date` — only when explicitly available; never inferred.
- `retrieved_at` — UTC retrieval timestamp when the pipeline fetched the page.

All new fields have defaults, so existing LLM responses and historical artifacts remain readable.

### Pipeline coverage

`src/graph/state.py` now includes `source_coverage`.

`src/graph/nodes.py` now:

- Classifies URLs deterministically by host/path.
- Records a UTC retrieval timestamp for successful page fetches.
- Creates coverage records for search-result URLs and follow-up-only pages.
- Labels observed auth-wall markers as `partial_public` without attempting authentication.
- Enriches LLM-selected memo sources with pipeline-observed metadata instead of allowing the model to invent retrieval facts.
- Prints provenance metadata in `researcher_memo.md`.

The implementation does **not** claim that a URL belongs to the target company. `identity_status` remains `unverified` unless later evidence-resolution logic explicitly upgrades it.

## Tests

Command:

```bash
PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/pytest -q
```

Result:

```text
5 passed, 1 warning
```

The warning is an existing third-party `langsmith` deprecation warning (`ast.Str`), not caused by this change.

Additional checks:

- Python compilation passed: `python -m compileall -q src tests`
- `git diff --check` passed.
- No Crawl4AI, Firecrawl, or external repository source was modified.

## ORCHVATE smoke benchmark

Command used a bounded `brief` run with `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY` and `TAVILY_API_KEY` empty, using the existing `openai/gpt-5-mini` model configuration.

Result: **completed successfully**.

Observed output:

- Official website identified: `https://www.orchvate.com/`.
- Public LinkedIn and Facebook profiles were included in the memo.
- Provenance lines were emitted for the website, Facebook, and LinkedIn.
- Facebook and LinkedIn were labeled `partial_public` because retrieved content contained login/guest-access markers.
- Retrieved timestamps were recorded for fetched social pages.
- The report correctly stated that no fetched source verified a UK Companies House entity and that absence of evidence is not proof of non-existence.
- PDF export remained non-fatal and was skipped because `xelatex` is not installed.

The smoke run also demonstrated the current limitation that registry coverage is still generic-search based; official registry connectors are deferred to P3.

## Files changed

- `src/schemas/research.py`
- `src/graph/state.py`
- `src/graph/nodes.py`
- `tests/test_source_provenance.py`

## Files intentionally not changed

- Crawl4AI source or installation.
- Firecrawl source checkout.
- Search-provider credentials or connector configuration.
- Database or persistent company-intelligence schema.
- Authentication, cookies, CAPTCHA handling, or access-control bypass logic.

## Next recommended phase

P1 should continue with typed claims and contradiction records, then P2 can add deterministic social-platform query templates and safe outbound-link discovery. P3 should add an authoritative Companies House connector. PostgreSQL should remain deferred until the structured evidence model and benchmark metrics demonstrate that filesystem/session storage is insufficient.

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p1_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p1/reports/orchvate_social_discovery_audit.md =====
# DeepResearchAgent Social-Media Discovery Audit

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Revision audited:** `604a79a`

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE; target jurisdiction: United Kingdom

**Audit type:** Read-only. No source files were modified.

## Executive summary

The existing repository has a **generic open-web research workflow**, not a social-media discovery subsystem. Its planner asks an LLM for a small list of ordinary web-search queries; its search adapters return generic `{title, url, snippet}` results; and its fetcher sends selected result URLs to Firecrawl or Crawl4AI. There are no platform-specific connectors, platform coverage fields, social-profile entity-resolution rules, registry APIs, cookie/browser-profile handling, or login/access-status schema.

The previous ORCHVATE run demonstrates the following:

- **LinkedIn:** discovered and directly fetched. The company page was accessible only in a public/guest form with sign-in/auth-wall elements. It was corroborated by the company website and had high identity confidence.
- **Facebook:** an official-site footer link was found, but the workflow did not follow or fetch the profile. The URL is evidence of an outbound association claim, not proof that the profile is currently controlled by ORCHVATE.
- **Instagram:** same as Facebook—linked from the official site, not directly fetched or independently verified.
- **X/Twitter:** no company profile URL was found in the existing run artifacts or cached ORCHVATE pages.
- **YouTube:** no ORCHVATE channel URL was found. A page contains the word “YouTuber” in a team/profile context, but that is not evidence of a company channel.
- **TikTok:** no company profile URL was found.
- **Company website/blog:** directly fetched when returned as search results. The site exposes a `/blog.html` link and blog-like pages such as `OurInsights.html` and `SuccessStory.html`, but the fetcher does not automatically crawl all outbound links from a fetched page.
- **Official registries:** the planner generated a Companies House-oriented query, but this was still a general search-engine query. There is no official registry connector or authoritative no-match verification.

## Existing workflow and relevant code paths

### Search planning

- `src/graph/nodes.py:338` — `plan_search_node()` asks the LLM for a fixed number of short web-search queries.
- The prompt requests a general overview, technical-depth, and recent-news/data query. It does **not** require a platform checklist, one query per social network, a registry query, or a source-type coverage matrix.
- `src/graph/nodes.py:459` — `follow_up_searches_node()` asks the LLM to generate gap-filling queries after initial fetching. It can produce a query such as an observed LinkedIn query, but platform coverage is not deterministic or required.

### Search execution

- `src/graph/nodes.py:375` — `execute_searches_node()` runs generated queries and deduplicates URLs.
- `src/tools/web_search.py:3-7,85-100` — search priority is Tavily, then Firecrawl, then DDGS. All providers expose a generic query interface; no platform-specific API or domain allowlist is implemented.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/ddgs_search.py:15-111` — DDGS executes ordinary text search and maps results to generic `SearchItem` objects.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/tavily_search.py:17-101` — Tavily does the same through its general search API.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/firecrawl_search.py` — Firecrawl search is also generic web search; it is not a social-platform connector.

### Fetching

- `src/graph/nodes.py:429` — `fetch_pages_node()` fetches only URLs already present in search results. It does not extract and enqueue outbound links from fetched pages.
- `src/utils/url_utils.py:95-115` — `fetch_crawl4ai_url()` calls `AsyncWebCrawler().arun(url=url)` with a 15-second timeout.
- `src/utils/url_utils.py:117-169` — `fetch_url()` uses Firecrawl when a key exists, then Crawl4AI, then a PDF fallback. It has no platform-specific status handling.
- `src/tools/web_fetch.py:17-53` — the public fetch tool returns only `url`, `title`, `content`, and `success`.

### State and evidence schemas

- `src/graph/state.py:32-35` — state stores generic `search_plan`, `search_results`, and `fetched_pages`.
- `src/schemas/research.py:35-40` — `ResearchSource` contains only title, URL, and evidence bullets.
- `src/schemas/research.py:43-104` — `ResearchMemo` has source quality notes and open questions but no platform, access status, discovery method, profile-ownership evidence, or registry-record type.
- `src/schemas/research.py:107-180` — `WriterReport` contains generic sections, claims, confidence, and citations but no required social-platform coverage matrix.

### Graph wiring

- `src/graph/graph.py:77-127` wires `detect_mode → plan_search → execute_searches → fetch_pages → follow_up_searches → build_memo → build_report → verify_claims → critique → save_artifacts`.
- There is no `discover_social_profiles`, `extract_outbound_links`, `verify_registry`, or `platform_access_check` node.

## Platform-by-platform findings

Legend:

- **Explicit planner search:** the workflow has a deterministic platform-specific query or connector requirement.
- **General-search discovery:** a generic search may return a platform URL, but the workflow does not require it.
- **Direct crawl:** the URL was passed to the fetcher and content was collected.
- **Access status:** whether public content is likely available without login/cookies/API, based only on observed behavior and implementation—not an attempt to bypass restrictions.

| **Company website** | **No platform-specific requirement; generic web search can find it.** | **Yes** | **Yes** — official pages were fetched with Crawl4AI. | Public pages were collected successfully. No login was required for the pages in the run. | [orchvate.com](https://www.orchvate.com/), plus `OurInsights.html`, `Enterprises.html`, `Neurodivergents.html`, and `SuccessStory.html`. The site links to `/blog.html` and exposes the Facebook, Instagram, and LinkedIn URLs in its footer. | **High** for the official domain as the brand’s public website. |
| **Company blog** | **No** | **Yes** | **Partially.** Blog-like pages were fetched when selected as search results; `/blog.html` was visible as an outbound link but was not automatically enqueued. | Public same-domain content; no login issue observed. Coverage depends on search-result selection. | [Our Insights](https://www.orchvate.com/OurInsights.html), [Success Story](https://www.orchvate.com/SuccessStory.html), and the site’s [blog index](https://www.orchvate.com/blog.html) link. | **High** for same-domain blog association; **partial** for coverage of all posts. |
| **Official company registries** | **No registry connector.** The observed planner included `Orchvate site:gov.uk Companies House`, but this is an ordinary search query. | **Yes** | **No authoritative registry page was fetched.** | No Companies House API/client, registry-specific URL policy, or explicit distinction between “no match,” “search failed,” and “not searched.” No access bypass was attempted. | No authoritative registry URL or registration record was collected. The previous report correctly marked UK legal registration as unverified. | **None for a legal entity/registration number.** |

## Evidence and identity caution

The following evidence levels should not be conflated:

1. **Official-site outbound link:** proves that the ORCHVATE website published a link to a profile URL. It does not prove the profile is active, controlled by the company today, or legally tied to a UK entity.
2. **Profile content:** a fetched profile can provide name, logo, website, address, and descriptions, but those fields may be self-managed and should be checked for consistency.
3. **Independent corroboration:** matching domain, phone, address, staff, or registry data can strengthen identity confidence.
4. **Official registry record:** only a first-party registry record can support a legal-entity name/number conclusion; a search-engine snippet is not sufficient.

The existing `ResearchSource` and `ClaimRecord` structures do not force the agent to record these distinctions. They allow evidence bullets and confidence prose, but not a typed association status such as `linked_from_official_site`, `profile_fetched`, `profile_verified`, or `registry_verified`.

## Access-control assessment

The repository does not attempt to bypass login requirements, CAPTCHA, cookies, or access controls. That is appropriate and should remain the policy.

Observed/implemented behavior:

- **LinkedIn:** Crawl4AI returned guest/public HTML but also exposed sign-in/auth-wall content. The agent must label this as partial public access rather than complete profile access.
- **Instagram/Facebook/X/TikTok/YouTube:** the generic fetcher may attempt public URLs if they appear in search results, but there is no platform-specific handling for JavaScript rendering, rate limits, consent pages, login walls, or API-only fields.
- **No authenticated browser profile:** no code path configures a user’s cookies/session or asks for browser takeover.
- **No official social APIs:** no OAuth clients or platform API keys are defined.
- **No CAPTCHA bypass:** none observed, and none should be added.

## Main gaps

1. **Planner coverage is probabilistic.** Social platforms are not required search targets. A good LLM may generate a LinkedIn query; another run may omit all social networks.
2. **No outbound-link discovery.** The crawler collects page content but does not parse links and enqueue official social/registry URLs.
3. **No platform metadata.** Search and fetch results lose the platform/source type, discovery path, access status, and retrieval outcome.
4. **No profile ownership verification.** The report can cite a URL but cannot systematically compare profile name, website, logo, address, phone, or linked domains to the target company.
5. **No registry integration.** Companies House is searched indirectly through general web search and never verified through a first-party result/API.
6. **No coverage completeness or negative-result semantics.** “Not found,” “not searched,” “search timed out,” “blocked,” and “requires login” are not separate states.
7. **No link-following budget/policy.** The official site exposes Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn, team-member LinkedIn links, and a blog index, but the fetcher does not selectively follow these links.
8. **No social-specific report section.** `WriterReport` does not require a platform matrix, profile URL, evidence link, identity confidence, or access limitation for each platform.
9. **Generic caching can blur benchmark provenance.** Search/page caches are shared across runs, so a strict audit should support a fresh cache or `--no-cache` mode.

## Smallest changes needed for broader public social coverage

These changes can be added without modifying Crawl4AI’s core crawler:

### 1. Require a platform checklist in `plan_search_node`

In `src/graph/nodes.py:338`, extend the planning prompt so company/social tasks produce a bounded set of queries for:

- LinkedIn;
- Instagram;
- Facebook;
- X/Twitter;
- YouTube;
- TikTok;
- official website/blog;
- target-jurisdiction registry.

Keep the number of queries bounded by depth. This is the smallest change, but it remains dependent on LLM compliance.

### 2. Add deterministic query templates

Add a small helper near `plan_search_node` that creates exact site-restricted queries such as:

- `site:linkedin.com/company ORCHVATE`
- `site:instagram.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:facebook.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:x.com ORCHVATE OR site:twitter.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:youtube.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:tiktok.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:gov.uk/companies-house ORCHVATE`

Treat these as discovery queries only, not proof of ownership or registration.

### 3. Extract and enqueue outbound links from fetched official pages

After `fetch_pages_node()` receives a page, parse same-domain and explicitly allowlisted social/registry links. Enqueue only relevant links, with a small budget and deduplication. This would surface the existing Facebook, Instagram, and LinkedIn footer links even when search engines omit them.

Do not blindly crawl every outbound link; use an allowlist for social hosts and jurisdiction-specific registry hosts.

### 4. Extend result/evidence schemas

Add fields to `SearchItem`/`ResearchSource` or a new typed record:

```text
platform: website | blog | linkedin | instagram | facebook | x | youtube | tiktok | registry | other
discovery_method: search | official_site_link | follow_up | direct_registry_query
access_status: fetched | partial_public | login_required | blocked | timeout | not_attempted
identity_status: unverified | possible | corroborated | rejected
identity_evidence: []
```

This is necessary to satisfy the requested audit output reliably.

### 5. Add a social-coverage report section

Require `WriterReport` to include one row per requested platform, even when no account is found. Each row should contain:

- URL or `null`;
- discovery method;
- fetched/not fetched;
- access status;
- evidence linking it to the company;
- confidence;
- explicit statement that absence is not proof of non-existence.

### 6. Add a first-party registry provider separately

For UK due diligence, implement a Companies House provider rather than relying on general search. It must distinguish:

- verified match;
- authoritative no-match;
- query failed;
- blocked/rate-limited;
- insufficient name coverage.

This is essential for legal-entity conclusions and should not be treated as ordinary social discovery.

## Final verdict

The existing DeepResearchAgent can discover public social profiles **opportunistically through generic search** and can directly fetch a public URL returned by search. It does not currently provide reliable, comprehensive social-media discovery.

For ORCHVATE specifically, the existing evidence supports:

- **High confidence:** official website and LinkedIn company-page association.
- **Medium confidence:** Facebook and Instagram URLs are officially linked from the ORCHVATE website, but the profiles were not fetched or independently verified.
- **No profile evidence in this run:** X/Twitter, YouTube, TikTok.
- **Unverified:** UK legal registration, because no first-party registry result was collected.

No source files were modified during this audit; the repository working tree remained clean at revision `604a79a`.

# Final Report

## Question

Investigate ORCHVATE as a company in the United Kingdom. Identify the official website, public social profiles, hashtags and mentions, and whether a UK legal entity is verified. Cite evidence and state what remains unverified.

## Executive Summary

The single most important finding is that the supplied public evidence confirms an Orchvate company profile on LinkedIn (which links to http://www.orchvate.com) and an Instagram account at https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/, but does not include any UK government registration (e.g., Companies House) to verify a UK legal entity. Overall confidence in the presence of the LinkedIn and Instagram profiles is High; confidence that there is no UK legal entity is Medium because the supplied materials simply lack government registry records rather than explicitly disproving registration. The key open uncertainty is whether Orchvate is registered in the UK (Companies House or other UK regulatory filings) and what the content of the linked corporate website (orchvate.com) states about country of registration.

## Public online presence and social profiles

Orchvate has an identifiable LinkedIn company page that lists a corporate website (http://www.orchvate.com), a Bengaluru, India headquarters and organizational metadata such as “Founded 2019,” company size “11-50 employees,” and follower counts (2,969 followers); the page also advertises workforce composition ("With a 90% neurodivergent workforce") and specialties in neurodiversity and data annotation [1]. An Instagram account with the username orchvate exists at https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/, but the retrieved Instagram HTML shell required login to view posts and profile content so post-level details (hashtags, mentions, or content confirming geography) were not accessible from the supplied source [2]. The supplied evidence set does not include fetched pages for Twitter/X, Facebook, or the orchvate.com site content itself, so the existence of additional official social profiles or the specific content of the corporate website remains unverified in this dataset [1][2].

**Key Statistics**

- LinkedIn follower count: 2,969 followers [1]
- Founded: 2019 [1]
- Company size on LinkedIn: 11-50 employees [1]
- Claimed workforce composition: 90% neurodivergent [1]

**Claims**

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | High | Strong agreement | Orchvate maintains an official LinkedIn company page that lists http://www.orchvate.com as its website. |
| 2 | High | Strong agreement | An Instagram account with username 'orchvate' exists at https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/. |
| 3 | High | Strong agreement | The LinkedIn page asserts specific organizational claims (90% neurodivergent workforce; specialties; Bengaluru HQ) but these claims are self-published and not independently corroborated by the supplied sources. |
| 4 | High | Insufficient evidence | No evidence for official Orchvate profiles on Twitter/X or Facebook was supplied in the provided dataset. |

### Claim 1: Orchvate maintains an official LinkedIn company page that lists http://www.orchv…

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

**Supporting Evidence**

- LinkedIn company page shows a 'Website' link to http://www.orchvate.com and contains organizational metadata (Founded 2019; Company size 11-50; 2,969 followers) [1].

### Claim 2: An Instagram account with username 'orchvate' exists at https://www.instagram.co…

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

**Supporting Evidence**

- Fetched Instagram profile URL exists and was retrieved (page shell indicates account username 'orchvate'); page required login to view posts [2].

### Claim 3: The LinkedIn page asserts specific organizational claims (90% neurodivergent wor…

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

**Supporting Evidence**

- LinkedIn 'About' text states 'With a 90% neurodivergent workforce' and lists specialties and an address in Bengaluru, Karnataka (postal code 560102) [1].
- Source is a company-managed LinkedIn profile, which is a primary but not independent source for legal or incorporation status [1].

### Claim 4: No evidence for official Orchvate profiles on Twitter/X or Facebook was supplied…

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Insufficient evidence

**Supporting Evidence**

- Only LinkedIn and Instagram pages were fetched in the supplied sources; there are no fetched Twitter/X or Facebook pages in the provided material to confirm presence on those platforms [1][2].

## UK legal entity status and verifiability

The supplied sources do not include any UK government registry records (such as Companies House filings) or other official UK incorporation documentation for Orchvate; therefore a UK legal entity cannot be confirmed from the provided material. The LinkedIn page lists employees and some UK-linked individuals appear in employee lists, but LinkedIn profile links alone do not constitute registry evidence of a UK-incorporated company [1]. The Instagram page provides no company registration data and required login for content access in the retrieved HTML shell, so it supplies no independent verification of UK legal presence [2]. To determine legal status in the UK would require targeted searches of Companies House, gov.uk records, or the company's own website content (orchvate.com), none of which were included in the supplied fetches.

**Key Statistics**

- No Companies House or gov.uk registration documents present in supplied sources [source set].

**Claims**

| # | Confidence | Source Agreement | Claim |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | High | Insufficient evidence | There is no supplied evidence of Orchvate being registered as a UK legal entity (e.g., Companies House filings) in the fetched materials. |
| 2 | Medium | Mixed | LinkedIn lists some UK-associated employees but those listings do not verify UK incorporation or officer status. |
| 3 | High | Insufficient evidence | The content of the domain orchvate.com (linked from LinkedIn) was not fetched in the supplied sources and thus its statements about registration, contact addresses, or legal domicile remain unverified. |

### Claim 1: There is no supplied evidence of Orchvate being registered as a UK legal entity …

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Insufficient evidence

**Supporting Evidence**

- Neither the LinkedIn nor the Instagram source provided or linked to Companies House or gov.uk registration documents in the supplied dataset [1][2].
- Source-quality notes state no Companies House / gov.uk registration document was supplied, so UK legal entity status remains unverified in the provided material.

### Claim 2: LinkedIn lists some UK-associated employees but those listings do not verify UK …

- **Confidence**: Medium
- **Source Agreement**: Mixed

**Supporting Evidence**

- LinkedIn employee section lists individuals including some with UK LinkedIn profiles (e.g., Dr Ian McDonald, Natalie Nurock) linked from the company page, but employee location/profile does not equal corporate registration [1].

### Claim 3: The content of the domain orchvate.com (linked from LinkedIn) was not fetched in…

- **Confidence**: High
- **Source Agreement**: Insufficient evidence

**Supporting Evidence**

- LinkedIn displays the website link (http://www.orchvate.com) but the orchvate.com site content was not included among the fetched sources, so any claims on that site could not be evaluated [1].

## Recommendations

- Search Companies House (find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk) for 'Orchvate' and likely variants (Orchvate Ltd, Orchvate LLP) — Researcher or due-diligence team should do this to confirm legal registration, because government registry data is the authoritative source for UK company legal status and none was present in the supplied material.
- Fetch and review the corporate website at http://www.orchvate.com — Researcher should retrieve the site content (including contact, company registration statements, and jurisdiction notices) because LinkedIn links to that domain but its content was not supplied and may contain incorporation details or contact addresses.
- Search X/Twitter and Facebook for 'Orchvate' and common variants, and collect public posts/hashtags — Communications or social-media analyst should perform these searches to identify additional official profiles, hashtags, or mentions, because only LinkedIn and Instagram were supplied and Instagram posts were not accessible without login.
- If UK registration documents are required for formal verification, contact named UK-based employees or representatives (via LinkedIn InMail or corporate contact from the website) and request evidence of UK incorporation or local tax/registration numbers — Compliance or legal counsel should act, because employee profiles alone do not prove legal domicile and direct documentary evidence (Companies House entry, VAT/GST registration) is needed.
- If time-sensitive verification is needed, commission a UK company search report (third-party commercial provider) — Procurement or legal teams can obtain a certificate of incumbency or company report more quickly than manual searches if required for contracting or onboarding decisions.

## Methodology & Confidence

### Methodology

Research used two supplied sources: a LinkedIn company page and an Instagram profile (both retrieved on 2026-10-09). Both are social-media sources and represent primary, company-managed content (LinkedIn) and a login-gated Instagram shell. No Companies House, gov.uk, company website content, X/Twitter, Facebook, news articles, or independent registry documents were provided or fetched. Date range of retrieval: 2026-10-09. Notable gaps: absence of government registry data for the UK, no fetched content from orchvate.com, and no fetched Twitter/X or Facebook pages; these gaps limit ability to verify UK legal status and to collect public hashtags/mentions from the company's posts.

### Overall Confidence

Well-supported conclusions: the existence of an Orchvate LinkedIn company page that links to http://www.orchvate.com and an Instagram account at https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/ are well-supported by the supplied sources (High confidence) because both pages were fetched or referenced directly [1][2]. Tentative conclusions: that Orchvate is or is not a UK-registered legal entity are tentative — the supplied materials lack Companies House/gov.uk records, so absence of evidence in this dataset is not proof of non-registration (Medium confidence). What would change the assessment: locating a Companies House entry or an explicit Companies House number on the orchvate.com site or other authoritative UK filings would confirm UK registration; conversely, an explicit statement on the corporate website that the entity is solely incorporated in India (and documentary evidence) would strengthen the assessment that there is no UK legal entity.

## Open Questions

- Is Orchvate (or any variant corporate name) registered at Companies House in the UK? (No Companies House records were supplied.)
- What content (contact address, incorporation jurisdiction, registration numbers) is present on http://www.orchvate.com and does it confirm a UK legal presence?
- Are there official Orchvate pages on Twitter/X or Facebook and what hashtags/mentions do they use?
- Do named UK-based LinkedIn employees hold corporate officer roles that would indicate a UK-incorporated subsidiary or branch?

## Sources

[1] Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate — https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate
[2] Fetched content from https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/ — https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/

## Report Notes

- Claims are separated from evidence so reviewers can inspect support independently.
- Confidence reflects strength and completeness of available evidence.
- Source Agreement reflects whether cited sources align, conflict, or remain too sparse.

# Researcher Memo

## Question

Investigate ORCHVATE as a company in the United Kingdom. Identify the official website, public social profiles, hashtags and mentions, and whether a UK legal entity is verified. Cite evidence and state what remains unverified.

## Task Mode

standard

## Search Plan

- ORCHVATE UK official website social media ORCHVATE LinkedIn Twitter Instagram Facebook hashtags
- ORCHVATE Companies House site:find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk OR site:gov.uk company registration
- site:linkedin.com/company ORCHVATE
- site:instagram.com ORCHVATE
- site:facebook.com ORCHVATE
- site:x.com ORCHVATE OR site:twitter.com ORCHVATE

## Summary

Available public evidence (from the supplied pages) confirms an Orchvate company profile on LinkedIn that links to http://www.orchvate.com and an Instagram account at https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/. LinkedIn supplies multiple company details (location in Bengaluru, India; founded 2019; company size 11-50; follower counts; stated 90% neurodivergent workforce). The Instagram URL exists but page content requires login to view. There is no supplied evidence from Companies House or other UK government registries to verify a UK legal entity for Orchvate; that remains unverified in the provided material. Other social profiles (X/Twitter, Facebook) and the contents of the claimed official website (orchvate.com) were not fetched in the provided sources and therefore are not independently confirmed here.

## Key Statistics

- With a 90% neurodivergent workforce — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate
- 2,969 followers — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate
- Company size 11-50 employees — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate
- Founded 2019 — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate
- View all 124 employees (text shown on page) — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate
- Address postal code 560102 (Bengaluru, Karnataka 560102, IN) — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate

## Key Quotes

- "Workplace for Everyone. Breaking Boundaries." — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate
- "We are a women-led, IIM-B incubated, DPIIT-recognized social enterprise creating sustainable employment pathways for neurodivergents." — Fetched content from https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate

## Source Quality Notes

Both supplied sources are social media profiles (LinkedIn and Instagram). The LinkedIn company page is a self-published corporate profile (marketing/organizational data) and provides multiple concrete data points but is not an independent government verification of legal status. The Instagram URL exists but page content requires login and thus could not be fully inspected from the fetched material. No peer-reviewed, news, Companies House, or other official government registration documents were provided; therefore there is a recency/verification gap regarding legal entity status in the UK. Potential bias: LinkedIn content is company-controlled and intended for marketing/recruiting; treat corporate claims (e.g., workforce composition, founding details) as primary-source assertions needing corroboration from official registries or the corporate website.

[32m+[m[32mdef _source_platform(url: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Classify a URL without making ownership or identity claims."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    parsed = urlparse(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m    host = parsed.netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")[m
[32m+[m[32m    path = parsed.path.lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("linkedin.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "linkedin"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("instagram.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "instagram"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("facebook.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "facebook"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host in {"x.com", "twitter.com", "t.co"} or host.endswith("twitter.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "x"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("youtube.com") or host == "youtu.be":[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "youtube"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("tiktok.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "tiktok"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if "companieshouse.gov.uk" in host or ("gov.uk" in host and "company" in path):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "registry"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if "blog" in path or "blog" in host:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "blog"[m
[32m+[m[32m    return "website"[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _access_status(content: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Describe observed public access without attempting authentication."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    text = (content or "").lower()[m

[32m+[m[32m    return "fetched"[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _enrich_memo_sources(memo_dict: dict, state: ResearchState) -> dict:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Attach pipeline-observed provenance to LLM-selected sources; never infer ownership."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    coverage = {item["url"]: item for item in state.get("source_coverage", []) if item.get("url")}[m
[32m+[m[32m    fetched = {item.get("url"): item for item in state.get("fetched_pages", []) if item.get("url")}[m
[32m+[m[32m    for source in memo_dict.get("sources", []):[m
[32m+[m[32m        url = source.get("url", "")[m
[32m+[m[32m        observed = coverage.get(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m        if observed:[m
[32m+[m[32m            source.update({k: v for k, v in observed.items() if v is not None})[m
[32m+[m[32m        else:[m
[32m+[m[32m            platform = _source_platform(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m            source.update([m
[32m+[m[32m                {[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "platform": platform,[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "source_category": _source_category(platform),[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "access_status": _access_status(fetched[url].get("content", "")) if url in fetched else "not_attempted",[m
[32m+[m[32m                    "discovery_method": "unknown",[m
[32m+[m[32m                }[m
[32m+[m[32m            )[m
[32m+[m[32m        source.setdefault("identity_status", "unverified")[m
[32m+[m[32m        if url in fetched and fetched[url].get("retrieved_at"):[m
[32m+[m[32m            source["retrieved_at"] = fetched[url]["retrieved_at"][m
[32m+[m[32m    return memo_dict[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _company_search_term(task: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Extract a compact company token for deterministic discovery queries."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    uppercase = re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Z0-9&.-]{2,}\b", task)[m
[32m+[m[32m    if uppercase:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return uppercase[0][m
[32m+[m[32m    quoted = re.findall(r"[\"']([^\"']{2,80})[\"']", task)[m
[32m+[m[32m    if quoted:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return quoted[0].strip()[m
[32m+[m[32m    return task.strip()[:80][m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _is_company_task(task: str) -> bool:[m
[32m+[m[32m    text = task.lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m    markers = ([m
[32m+[m[32m        "company", "legal entity", "corporate", "business", "organization",[m
[32m+[m[32m        "official website", "social profile", "registry", "company intelligence",[m
[32m+[m[32m    )[m
[32m+[m[32m    return any(marker in text for marker in markers)[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _deterministic_social_queries(task: str, depth: str) -> List[str]:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Return bounded, provider-neutral public discovery queries for company tasks."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    if not _is_company_task(task):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return [][m
[32m+[m[32m    term = _company_search_term(task)[m
[32m+[m[32m    queries = [[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:linkedin.com/company {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:instagram.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:facebook.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:x.com {term} OR site:twitter.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:youtube.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:tiktok.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company {term} Companies House",[m
[32m+[m[32m    ][m
[32m+[m[32m    budget = {"brief": 4, "standard": 6, "comprehensive": len(queries)}.get(depth, 6)[m
[32m+[m[32m    return queries[:budget][m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _extract_followable_links(pages: List[dict], max_links: int = 8) -> List[str]:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Extract only same-site or explicitly allowlisted public links from official pages."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    priority_links: List[str] = [][m
[32m+[m[32m    same_site_links: List[str] = [][m
[32m+[m[32m    seen: set[str] = set()[m
[32m+[m[32m    for page in pages:[m
[32m+[m[32m        page_url = page.get("url", "")[m
[32m+[m[32m        page_host = urlparse(page_url).netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")[m
[32m+[m[32m        if not page_host or _source_platform(page_url) in {"linkedin", "instagram", "facebook", "x", "youtube", "tiktok", "registry"}:[m
[32m+[m[32m            continue[m
[32m+[m[32m        for raw in re.findall(r"https?://[^\s<>\]\)\"']+", page.get("content", "")):[m
[32m+[m[32m            candidate = raw.rstrip(".,;:!?")[m
[32m+[m[32m            parsed = urlparse(candidate)[m
[32m+[m[32m            host = parsed.netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")[m
[32m+[m[32m            allowed_external = _source_platform(candidate) in {[m
[32m+[m[32m                "linkedin", "instagram", "facebook", "x", "youtube", "tiktok", "registry"[m
[32m+[m[32m            }[m
[32m+[m[32m            if not parsed.scheme or not host or (host != page_host and not allowed_external):[m
[32m+[m[32m                continue[m
[32m+[m[32m            if candidate not in seen:[m
[32m+[m[32m                seen.add(candidate)[m
[32m+[m[32m                (priority_links if allowed_external else same_site_links).append(candidate)[m
[32m+[m[32m    return (priority_links + same_site_links)[:max_links][m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _extract_social_terms(pages: List[dict], max_terms: int = 8) -> List[str]:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Extract public hashtag/mention tokens; matching is not treated as ownership proof."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    terms: List[str] = [][m
[32m+[m[32m    seen: set[str] = set()[m
[32m+[m[32m    for page in pages:[m
[32m+[m[32m        for token in re.findall(r"(?<!\w)[#@][A-Za-z0-9_]{2,50}", page.get("content", "")):[m
[32m+[m[32m            normalized = token.lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m            if normalized not in seen:[m
[32m+[m[32m                seen.add(normalized)[m
[32m+[m[32m                terms.append(token)[m
[32m+[m[32m                if len(terms) >= max_terms:[m
[32m+[m[32m                    return terms[m
[32m+[m[32m    return terms[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m

     """LLM for lightweight tasks: query planning, gap analysis, task detection."""[m
     return get_llm(state["model_name"])[m

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p2_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p2/patch/test_source_provenance.py =====
from src.graph.nodes import (
    _access_status,
    _coverage_records,
    _deterministic_social_queries,
    _enrich_memo_sources,
    _extract_followable_links,
    _extract_social_terms,
    _source_category,
    _source_platform,
)


def test_source_platform_classification():
    assert _source_platform("https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/") == "linkedin"
    assert _source_platform("https://instagram.com/orchvate") == "instagram"
    assert _source_platform("https://www.facebook.com/orchvate/") == "facebook"
    assert _source_platform("https://x.com/orchvate") == "x"
    assert _source_platform("https://www.youtube.com/@orchvate") == "youtube"
    assert _source_platform("https://www.tiktok.com/@orchvate") == "tiktok"
    assert _source_platform("https://find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/123") == "registry"
    assert _source_platform("https://example.com/blog.html") == "blog"
    assert _source_category("linkedin") == "social"
    assert _source_category("registry") == "registry"


def test_access_status_is_observational_and_does_not_authenticate():
    assert _access_status("Public company description") == "fetched"
    assert _access_status("Agree & Join\nSign in to see more") == "partial_public"


def test_coverage_records_distinguish_fetched_and_not_attempted():
    search_results = [
        {"url": "https://example.com", "title": "Example"},
        {"url": "https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/", "title": "Orchvate"},
    ]
    fetched_pages = [
        {"url": "https://example.com", "title": "Example", "content": "OK", "retrieved_at": "now"},
    ]
    records = {row["url"]: row for row in _coverage_records(search_results, fetched_pages)}
    assert records["https://example.com"]["access_status"] == "fetched"
    assert records["https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/"]["access_status"] == "not_attempted"


def test_memo_sources_receive_pipeline_provenance_without_identity_inference():
    memo = {
        "sources": [
            {"title": "Orchvate", "url": "https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/", "evidence": ["name"]}
        ]
    }
    state = {
        "source_coverage": [
            {
                "url": "https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/",
                "platform": "linkedin",
                "source_category": "social",
                "discovery_method": "search_result",
                "access_status": "partial_public",
                "retrieved_at": "now",
            }
        ],
        "fetched_pages": [],
    }
    enriched = _enrich_memo_sources(memo, state)
    source = enriched["sources"][0]
    assert source["platform"] == "linkedin"
    assert source["access_status"] == "partial_public"
    assert source["identity_status"] == "unverified"


def test_deterministic_social_queries_are_bounded_to_company_tasks():
    queries = _deterministic_social_queries(
        "Investigate ORCHVATE as a company in the United Kingdom", "comprehensive"
    )
    assert any("linkedin.com/company" in query for query in queries)
    assert any("instagram.com" in query for query in queries)
    assert any("Companies House" in query for query in queries)
    assert _deterministic_social_queries("Compare two software frameworks", "comprehensive") == []


def test_followable_links_prioritize_allowlisted_social_hosts():
    pages = [
        {
            "url": "https://orchvate.example/index.html",
            "content": (
                "[Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/orchvate/) "
                "[Other](https://unrelated.example/profile) "
                "[Blog](https://orchvate.example/blog.html)"
            ),
        }
    ]
    links = _extract_followable_links(pages, max_links=2)
    assert "https://www.facebook.com/orchvate/" in links
    assert "https://unrelated.example/profile" not in links


def test_social_terms_are_extracted_without_claiming_ownership():
    pages = [{"content": "We support #Neurodiversity and mention @orchvate."}]
    assert _extract_social_terms(pages) == ["#Neurodiversity", "@orchvate"]

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p2_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p2/reports/orchvate_p1_implementation_report.md =====
# Company Intelligence Agent — P1 Implementation Report

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Baseline revision:** `604a79a`

**Phase:** P1 — reliable research and evidence

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE, target jurisdiction United Kingdom

## Scope implemented

The first bounded P1 slice adds provenance and coverage metadata without introducing a database, new external provider, authentication, or platform-access bypass.

### Evidence schema

`src/schemas/research.py` → `ResearchSource` now supports backward-compatible fields for:

- `platform` — website, blog, LinkedIn, Instagram, Facebook, X, YouTube, TikTok, registry, or other.
- `source_category` — official, social, blog, registry, or web.
- `discovery_method` — search result, follow-up search, official-site link, direct registry query, or unknown.
- `access_status` — fetched, partial public, login required, blocked, timeout, not attempted, or unknown.
- `identity_status` — unverified, candidate, corroborated, conflicting, or rejected.
- `publication_date` — only when explicitly available; never inferred.
- `retrieved_at` — UTC retrieval timestamp when the pipeline fetched the page.

All new fields have defaults, so existing LLM responses and historical artifacts remain readable.

### Pipeline coverage

`src/graph/state.py` now includes `source_coverage`.

`src/graph/nodes.py` now:

- Classifies URLs deterministically by host/path.
- Records a UTC retrieval timestamp for successful page fetches.
- Creates coverage records for search-result URLs and follow-up-only pages.
- Labels observed auth-wall markers as `partial_public` without attempting authentication.
- Enriches LLM-selected memo sources with pipeline-observed metadata instead of allowing the model to invent retrieval facts.
- Prints provenance metadata in `researcher_memo.md`.

The implementation does **not** claim that a URL belongs to the target company. `identity_status` remains `unverified` unless later evidence-resolution logic explicitly upgrades it.

## Tests

Command:

```bash
PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/pytest -q
```

Result:

```text
5 passed, 1 warning
```

The warning is an existing third-party `langsmith` deprecation warning (`ast.Str`), not caused by this change.

Additional checks:

- Python compilation passed: `python -m compileall -q src tests`
- `git diff --check` passed.
- No Crawl4AI, Firecrawl, or external repository source was modified.

## ORCHVATE smoke benchmark

Command used a bounded `brief` run with `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY` and `TAVILY_API_KEY` empty, using the existing `openai/gpt-5-mini` model configuration.

Result: **completed successfully**.

Observed output:

- Official website identified: `https://www.orchvate.com/`.
- Public LinkedIn and Facebook profiles were included in the memo.
- Provenance lines were emitted for the website, Facebook, and LinkedIn.
- Facebook and LinkedIn were labeled `partial_public` because retrieved content contained login/guest-access markers.
- Retrieved timestamps were recorded for fetched social pages.
- The report correctly stated that no fetched source verified a UK Companies House entity and that absence of evidence is not proof of non-existence.
- PDF export remained non-fatal and was skipped because `xelatex` is not installed.

The smoke run also demonstrated the current limitation that registry coverage is still generic-search based; official registry connectors are deferred to P3.

## Files changed

- `src/schemas/research.py`
- `src/graph/state.py`
- `src/graph/nodes.py`
- `tests/test_source_provenance.py`

## Files intentionally not changed

- Crawl4AI source or installation.
- Firecrawl source checkout.
- Search-provider credentials or connector configuration.
- Database or persistent company-intelligence schema.
- Authentication, cookies, CAPTCHA handling, or access-control bypass logic.

P3 should add an authoritative Companies House connector. PostgreSQL should remain deferred until the structured evidence model and benchmark metrics demonstrate that filesystem/session storage is insufficient.
