# Company Intelligence Agent — P2 Implementation Report

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Baseline revision:** `604a79a`

**Phase:** P2 — broad public source discovery

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE, target jurisdiction United Kingdom

## Scope implemented

P2 adds bounded, provider-neutral public-source discovery on top of the P1 provenance layer.

### Deterministic social and registry queries

Company-intelligence tasks now receive deterministic site-restricted discovery queries in addition to the LLM-generated queries:

- LinkedIn company pages
- Instagram
- Facebook
- X/Twitter
- YouTube
- TikTok
- UK Companies House

The query budget is depth-aware:

- `brief`: first four social targets
- `standard`: six social targets
- `comprehensive`: all configured social targets plus the registry query

Non-company comparison or technical tasks do not receive these queries.

### Safe official-site link following

After initial pages are fetched, the pipeline extracts URLs from public page content and follows only:

- same-host links; or
- explicitly allowlisted public social and registry hosts.

External arbitrary links are rejected. Social and registry links are prioritized over ordinary same-site links within a small bounded budget. Links followed from official pages receive `official_site_link` provenance rather than being mislabeled as search results.

No login, cookie, CAPTCHA, or access-control bypass was added.

### Hashtag and mention expansion

Public `#hashtags` and `@mentions` found in retrieved pages are deduplicated and converted into a bounded portion of follow-up search queries. Query execution is recorded in `search_plan`.

A hashtag or mention match remains only a discovery signal; it does not establish ownership or relevance.

## ORCHVATE fresh-cache benchmark

The benchmark used:

- `openai/gpt-5-mini`
- `brief` depth
- Firecrawl and Tavily disabled
- a fresh isolated cache at `/tmp/orchvate-p2-cache`

Result: **completed successfully**.

Observed search plan included:

```text
ORCHVATE UK official website social media ORCHVATE LinkedIn Twitter Instagram Facebook hashtags
ORCHVATE Companies House site:find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk OR site:gov.uk company registration
site:linkedin.com/company ORCHVATE
site:instagram.com ORCHVATE
site:facebook.com ORCHVATE
site:x.com ORCHVATE OR site:twitter.com ORCHVATE
```

Observed direct fetches:

- `https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate`
- `https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/`

Observed memo provenance:

- LinkedIn: `platform=linkedin`, `discovery=search_result`, `access=partial_public`, `identity=corroborated`.
- Instagram: `platform=instagram`, `discovery=search_result`, `access=partial_public`, `identity=candidate`.
- UTC retrieval timestamps were recorded for both pages.

The report correctly stated that the run did not verify a UK legal entity and that missing registry evidence is not proof of non-existence.

The benchmark did not happen to select an official same-domain website page among the first fetch budget, so it did not demonstrate an `official_site_link` row in this particular run. The link-following behavior is covered by unit tests and will activate whenever a fetched official page contains eligible links.

PDF export remained non-fatal and was skipped because `xelatex` is not installed.

## Tests and checks

```text
8 tests passed, 1 existing third-party deprecation warning
compileall passed
git diff --check passed
```

## Files changed cumulatively from baseline

- `src/graph/nodes.py`
- `src/graph/state.py`
- `src/schemas/research.py`
- `tests/test_source_provenance.py`

## Safety and limitations

- Public discovery only; no authentication or access-control bypass.
- Search-engine result presence is not identity proof.
- Public social pages can be partial, dynamic, stale, or login-walled.
- Hashtag and mention matches are not ownership proof.
- Companies House remains general-search discovery at P2; authoritative registry verification is deferred to P3.
- No claim is made of complete internet or platform coverage.

## Next recommended phase

P3 should add authoritative registry connectors, typed company/person identity resolution, current-versus-historical role handling, dated events, contradiction records, and public business-contact provenance. PostgreSQL remains deferred until the evidence model and benchmark metrics justify persistent relational storage.

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p2_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p2/reports/orchvate_social_discovery_audit.md =====
# DeepResearchAgent Social-Media Discovery Audit

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Revision audited:** `604a79a`

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE; target jurisdiction: United Kingdom

**Audit type:** Read-only. No source files were modified.

## Executive summary

The existing repository has a **generic open-web research workflow**, not a social-media discovery subsystem. Its planner asks an LLM for a small list of ordinary web-search queries; its search adapters return generic `{title, url, snippet}` results; and its fetcher sends selected result URLs to Firecrawl or Crawl4AI. There are no platform-specific connectors, platform coverage fields, social-profile entity-resolution rules, registry APIs, cookie/browser-profile handling, or login/access-status schema.

The previous ORCHVATE run demonstrates the following:

- **LinkedIn:** discovered and directly fetched. The company page was accessible only in a public/guest form with sign-in/auth-wall elements. It was corroborated by the company website and had high identity confidence.
- **Facebook:** an official-site footer link was found, but the workflow did not follow or fetch the profile. The URL is evidence of an outbound association claim, not proof that the profile is currently controlled by ORCHVATE.
- **Instagram:** same as Facebook—linked from the official site, not directly fetched or independently verified.
- **X/Twitter:** no company profile URL was found in the existing run artifacts or cached ORCHVATE pages.
- **YouTube:** no ORCHVATE channel URL was found. A page contains the word “YouTuber” in a team/profile context, but that is not evidence of a company channel.
- **TikTok:** no company profile URL was found.
- **Company website/blog:** directly fetched when returned as search results. The site exposes a `/blog.html` link and blog-like pages such as `OurInsights.html` and `SuccessStory.html`, but the fetcher does not automatically crawl all outbound links from a fetched page.
- **Official registries:** the planner generated a Companies House-oriented query, but this was still a general search-engine query. There is no official registry connector or authoritative no-match verification.

## Existing workflow and relevant code paths

### Search planning

- `src/graph/nodes.py:338` — `plan_search_node()` asks the LLM for a fixed number of short web-search queries.
- The prompt requests a general overview, technical-depth, and recent-news/data query. It does **not** require a platform checklist, one query per social network, a registry query, or a source-type coverage matrix.
- `src/graph/nodes.py:459` — `follow_up_searches_node()` asks the LLM to generate gap-filling queries after initial fetching. It can produce a query such as an observed LinkedIn query, but platform coverage is not deterministic or required.

### Search execution

- `src/graph/nodes.py:375` — `execute_searches_node()` runs generated queries and deduplicates URLs.
- `src/tools/web_search.py:3-7,85-100` — search priority is Tavily, then Firecrawl, then DDGS. All providers expose a generic query interface; no platform-specific API or domain allowlist is implemented.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/ddgs_search.py:15-111` — DDGS executes ordinary text search and maps results to generic `SearchItem` objects.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/tavily_search.py:17-101` — Tavily does the same through its general search API.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/firecrawl_search.py` — Firecrawl search is also generic web search; it is not a social-platform connector.

### Fetching

- `src/graph/nodes.py:429` — `fetch_pages_node()` fetches only URLs already present in search results. It does not extract and enqueue outbound links from fetched pages.
- `src/utils/url_utils.py:95-115` — `fetch_crawl4ai_url()` calls `AsyncWebCrawler().arun(url=url)` with a 15-second timeout.
- `src/utils/url_utils.py:117-169` — `fetch_url()` uses Firecrawl when a key exists, then Crawl4AI, then a PDF fallback. It has no platform-specific status handling.
- `src/tools/web_fetch.py:17-53` — the public fetch tool returns only `url`, `title`, `content`, and `success`.

### State and evidence schemas

- `src/graph/state.py:32-35` — state stores generic `search_plan`, `search_results`, and `fetched_pages`.
- `src/schemas/research.py:35-40` — `ResearchSource` contains only title, URL, and evidence bullets.
- `src/schemas/research.py:43-104` — `ResearchMemo` has source quality notes and open questions but no platform, access status, discovery method, profile-ownership evidence, or registry-record type.
- `src/schemas/research.py:107-180` — `WriterReport` contains generic sections, claims, confidence, and citations but no required social-platform coverage matrix.

### Graph wiring

- `src/graph/graph.py:77-127` wires `detect_mode → plan_search → execute_searches → fetch_pages → follow_up_searches → build_memo → build_report → verify_claims → critique → save_artifacts`.
- There is no `discover_social_profiles`, `extract_outbound_links`, `verify_registry`, or `platform_access_check` node.

## Platform-by-platform findings

Legend:

- **Explicit planner search:** the workflow has a deterministic platform-specific query or connector requirement.
- **General-search discovery:** a generic search may return a platform URL, but the workflow does not require it.
- **Direct crawl:** the URL was passed to the fetcher and content was collected.
- **Access status:** whether public content is likely available without login/cookies/API, based only on observed behavior and implementation—not an attempt to bypass restrictions.

| Platform / source | Explicitly searched by planner? | Could be discovered only through general web search? | Directly crawled in existing ORCHVATE run? | Access / restriction status | ORCHVATE URL and evidence | Confidence that URL belongs to ORCHVATE |

No login/cookies/API were used. Full feed/profile access is restricted. | Profile URL: [LinkedIn company page](https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/). No Facebook page content was collected. | **Medium** as an official-site association claim; **not verified** as a current controlled/active page. |

Public X pages can be JavaScript-heavy and can impose login/rate limits; the implementation has no cookies/API/profile support. | No ORCHVATE X/Twitter URL was present in the previous search plan, final source list, or cached ORCHVATE page content reviewed. | **None for a profile**. This is absence of evidence in the run artifacts, not proof that no account exists. |

The cached site text includes “YouTuber” in a team/profile context, but no company-channel URL; that mention must not be promoted to a YouTube profile. | **None for a profile**. |

| **Company website** | **No platform-specific requirement; generic web search can find it.** | **Yes** | **Yes** — official pages were fetched with Crawl4AI. | Public pages were collected successfully. No login was required for the pages in the run. | [orchvate.com](https://www.orchvate.com/), plus `OurInsights.html`, `Enterprises.html`, `Neurodivergents.html`, and `SuccessStory.html`. The site links to `/blog.html` and exposes the Facebook, Instagram, and LinkedIn URLs in its footer. | **High** for the official domain as the brand’s public website. |
| **Company blog** | **No** | **Yes** | **Partially.** Blog-like pages were fetched when selected as search results; `/blog.html` was visible as an outbound link but was not automatically enqueued. | Public same-domain content; no login issue observed. Coverage depends on search-result selection. | [Our Insights](https://www.orchvate.com/OurInsights.html), [Success Story](https://www.orchvate.com/SuccessStory.html), and the site’s [blog index](https://www.orchvate.com/blog.html) link. | **High** for same-domain blog association; **partial** for coverage of all posts. |
| **Official company registries** | **No registry connector.** The observed planner included `Orchvate site:gov.uk Companies House`, but this is an ordinary search query. | **Yes** | **No authoritative registry page was fetched.** | No Companies House API/client, registry-specific URL policy, or explicit distinction between “no match,” “search failed,” and “not searched.” No access bypass was attempted. | No authoritative registry URL or registration record was collected. The previous report correctly marked UK legal registration as unverified. | **None for a legal entity/registration number.** |

## Evidence and identity caution

The following evidence levels should not be conflated:

1. **Official-site outbound link:** proves that the ORCHVATE website published a link to a profile URL. It does not prove the profile is active, controlled by the company today, or legally tied to a UK entity.
2. **Profile content:** a fetched profile can provide name, logo, website, address, and descriptions, but those fields may be self-managed and should be checked for consistency.
3. **Independent corroboration:** matching domain, phone, address, staff, or registry data can strengthen identity confidence.
4. **Official registry record:** only a first-party registry record can support a legal-entity name/number conclusion; a search-engine snippet is not sufficient.

The existing `ResearchSource` and `ClaimRecord` structures do not force the agent to record these distinctions. They allow evidence bullets and confidence prose, but not a typed association status such as `linked_from_official_site`, `profile_fetched`, `profile_verified`, or `registry_verified`.

## Access-control assessment

The repository does not attempt to bypass login requirements, CAPTCHA, cookies, or access controls. That is appropriate and should remain the policy.

Observed/implemented behavior:

- **LinkedIn:** Crawl4AI returned guest/public HTML but also exposed sign-in/auth-wall content. The agent must label this as partial public access rather than complete profile access.
- **Instagram/Facebook/X/TikTok/YouTube:** the generic fetcher may attempt public URLs if they appear in search results, but there is no platform-specific handling for JavaScript rendering, rate limits, consent pages, login walls, or API-only fields.
- **No authenticated browser profile:** no code path configures a user’s cookies/session or asks for browser takeover.
- **No official social APIs:** no OAuth clients or platform API keys are defined.
- **No CAPTCHA bypass:** none observed, and none should be added.

## Main gaps

1. **Planner coverage is probabilistic.** Social platforms are not required search targets. A good LLM may generate a LinkedIn query; another run may omit all social networks.
2. **No outbound-link discovery.** The crawler collects page content but does not parse links and enqueue official social/registry URLs.
3. **No platform metadata.** Search and fetch results lose the platform/source type, discovery path, access status, and retrieval outcome.
4. **No profile ownership verification.** The report can cite a URL but cannot systematically compare profile name, website, logo, address, phone, or linked domains to the target company.
5. **No registry integration.** Companies House is searched indirectly through general web search and never verified through a first-party result/API.
6. **No coverage completeness or negative-result semantics.** “Not found,” “not searched,” “search timed out,” “blocked,” and “requires login” are not separate states.
7. **No link-following budget/policy.** The official site exposes Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn, team-member LinkedIn links, and a blog index, but the fetcher does not selectively follow these links.
8. **No social-specific report section.** `WriterReport` does not require a platform matrix, profile URL, evidence link, identity confidence, or access limitation for each platform.
9. **Generic caching can blur benchmark provenance.** Search/page caches are shared across runs, so a strict audit should support a fresh cache or `--no-cache` mode.

## Smallest changes needed for broader public social coverage

These changes can be added without modifying Crawl4AI’s core crawler:

### 1. Require a platform checklist in `plan_search_node`

In `src/graph/nodes.py:338`, extend the planning prompt so company/social tasks produce a bounded set of queries for:

- LinkedIn;
- Instagram;
- Facebook;
- X/Twitter;
- YouTube;
- TikTok;
- official website/blog;
- target-jurisdiction registry.

Keep the number of queries bounded by depth. This is the smallest change, but it remains dependent on LLM compliance.

### 2. Add deterministic query templates

Add a small helper near `plan_search_node` that creates exact site-restricted queries such as:

- `site:linkedin.com/company ORCHVATE`
- `site:instagram.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:facebook.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:x.com ORCHVATE OR site:twitter.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:youtube.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:tiktok.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:gov.uk/companies-house ORCHVATE`

Treat these as discovery queries only, not proof of ownership or registration.

### 3. Extract and enqueue outbound links from fetched official pages

After `fetch_pages_node()` receives a page, parse same-domain and explicitly allowlisted social/registry links. Enqueue only relevant links, with a small budget and deduplication. This would surface the existing Facebook, Instagram, and LinkedIn footer links even when search engines omit them.

Do not blindly crawl every outbound link; use an allowlist for social hosts and jurisdiction-specific registry hosts.

### 4. Extend result/evidence schemas

Add fields to `SearchItem`/`ResearchSource` or a new typed record:

```text
platform: website | blog | linkedin | instagram | facebook | x | youtube | tiktok | registry | other
discovery_method: search | official_site_link | follow_up | direct_registry_query
access_status: fetched | partial_public | login_required | blocked | timeout | not_attempted
identity_status: unverified | possible | corroborated | rejected
identity_evidence: []
```

This is necessary to satisfy the requested audit output reliably.

### 5. Add a social-coverage report section

Require `WriterReport` to include one row per requested platform, even when no account is found. Each row should contain:

- URL or `null`;
- discovery method;
- fetched/not fetched;
- access status;
- evidence linking it to the company;
- confidence;
- explicit statement that absence is not proof of non-existence.

### 6. Add a first-party registry provider separately

For UK due diligence, implement a Companies House provider rather than relying on general search. It must distinguish:

- verified match;
- authoritative no-match;
- query failed;
- blocked/rate-limited;
- insufficient name coverage.

This is essential for legal-entity conclusions and should not be treated as ordinary social discovery.

## Final verdict

The existing DeepResearchAgent can discover public social profiles **opportunistically through generic search** and can directly fetch a public URL returned by search. It does not currently provide reliable, comprehensive social-media discovery.

For ORCHVATE specifically, the existing evidence supports:

- **High confidence:** official website and LinkedIn company-page association.
- **Medium confidence:** Facebook and Instagram URLs are officially linked from the ORCHVATE website, but the profiles were not fetched or independently verified.
- **No profile evidence in this run:** X/Twitter, YouTube, TikTok.
- **Unverified:** UK legal registration, because no first-party registry result was collected.

No source files were modified during this audit; the repository working tree remained clean at revision `604a79a`.

# Final Report

## Question

Investigate ORCHVATE as a company in the United Kingdom. Verify its UK legal entity if possible, resolve company and person identities, distinguish current and historical roles, extract dated events, contradictions, and publicly published business contacts. Cite evidence and state what remains unverified.

## Executive Summary

The single most important finding is that the supplied materials do not contain any company record, officer appointments, filings, or contact details that verify a UK-registered entity named ORCHVATE; this conclusion is well-supported by the fetched Companies House and GOV.UK pages. Overall confidence in that finding is High because the provided pages are authoritative registry interfaces and developer documentation but contain only generic landing/guidance content, not company-specific records [1][2][3]. The key open uncertainty is whether ORCHVATE exists in Companies House or other public sources outside the supplied materials — resolving that requires targeted searches, API queries (with a configured API key), or external social/business-registry checks.

## Registry verification and legal-entity status

A review of the supplied Companies House search/interface pages and GOV.UK Companies House pages shows only generic registry landing and guidance content; no company-specific page, officer list, filing images, or confirmation statements for an entity named ORCHVATE were included in the supplied material [1][2]. The developer documentation supplied confirms that an API-based check could be performed but the researcher’s registry_verification record shows the Companies House API key was not configured, which prevented automated lookups of matching names and document images in the supplied run [3].

**Key Statistics**

- 0 company-specific records for 'ORCHVATE' in the supplied materials
- 1 registry verification failure due to missing API key (COMPANIES_HOUSE_API_KEY not configured)

**Claims**

| # | Confidence | Source Agreement | Claim |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | High | Strong agreement | No company record for a UK-registered company named ORCHVATE appears in the supplied Companies House or GOV.UK pages. |
| 2 | High | Strong agreement | Automated verification via the Companies House API was not completed because the API key was not configured in the retrieval environment. |
| 3 | High | Strong agreement | No filings, officer appointment/resignation images, or registered-office entries for ORCHVATE were provided in the supplied material. |

- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

- The GOV.UK material supplied describes Companies House services but does not include company-specific filings for ORCHVATE in the provided content [2].

## Identity resolution, roles, contacts, and next steps

Because the supplied materials did not include a company record or filings, no named officers, persons of significant control (PSCs), or dated events (appointments/resignations) linking individuals to ORCHVATE could be established from the provided pages [1][2]. Likewise, no publicly published business contacts (company email, telephone, verified social accounts) for ORCHVATE were present in the fetched content, so identity resolution and role differentiation could not be completed from the current evidence set [1][2].

The most direct way to resolve these unknowns is to run targeted searches (exact and fuzzy name variants) on Companies House and to query the Companies House API with a configured key to retrieve matching company records and filing images; complementary searches of LinkedIn, X/Twitter, Instagram, Facebook, domain WHOIS, and other business registries should be performed to triangulate identities and published contacts if the registry search yields candidates [3].

**Key Statistics**

- 0 named officers or PSCs for ORCHVATE provided in the supplied material
- 0 published business contacts for ORCHVATE in the supplied material
- 6 open follow-up questions identified for further research

**Claims**

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | High | Strong agreement | No persons or officers can be linked to ORCHVATE from the supplied material; identity candidates were not provided. |
| 2 | High | Strong agreement | No publicly published business contacts for ORCHVATE were present in the supplied materials. |
| 3 | High | Strong agreement | Further targeted searches (Companies House UI and API with a configured key, social networks, WHOIS, other registries) are required to verify existence and resolve identities. |

- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

- The GOV.UK and Companies House landing/guidance pages in the supplied material do not include personnel or contact data for ORCHVATE [2].

- **Source Agreement**: Strong agreement

**Supporting Evidence**

- Companies House developer documentation indicates API endpoints that can return company records and filing images when queried programmatically; a configured API key would enable these lookups [3].
- The researcher’s summary recommends targeted Companies House searches and social/network registry checks as the next steps to resolve the open questions because the supplied materials lacked company-specific records.

- Registry outcome: inconclusive_verification
- Registry note: COMPANIES_HOUSE_API_KEY is not configured

## Recommendations

- Researcher: Perform targeted Companies House searches for 'ORCHVATE' and close variants (exact and fuzzy) using the Companies House web interface to identify any matching company detail pages; why: the supplied materials were only landing/guidance pages and may have omitted an existing record.
- Researcher / Technical operator: Configure and use a Companies House API key and query the company name endpoints (name matching, disambiguation) and download filing images for any candidate matches; why: the registry_verification metadata shows the API key was not configured, and the API can return officer appointment documents and filing images needed to verify identities and dated events [3].
- Researcher: Search social and business platforms (LinkedIn company pages, director profiles on LinkedIn, X/Twitter, Instagram, Facebook), WHOIS for candidate domains, and broader web/press archives for 'ORCHVATE' and name variants; why: if a Companies House record is absent or ambiguous, corroborating evidence from social or domain records can identify operating businesses and published contacts.
- Client / Subject (if available): If you control ORCHVATE, provide the Companies House company number, incorporation documents, or official contact details to expedite verification; why: direct documentation is the fastest route to resolve legal-entity and officer identity questions.

## Methodology & Confidence

### Methodology

I used the supplied researcher memo and the indicated fetched webpage types (Companies House find-and-update landing/interface, GOV.UK Companies House pages, and Companies House developer documentation). In total I cite three official source endpoints provided in the memo set: the Companies House search interface, GOV.UK Companies House organizational pages, and the Companies House developer docs. The retrieval timestamp in the memo is 2026-10-09, and some GOV.UK pages in the supplied material showed 2026 recency indicators. Notable gaps: the supplied material included only generic landing/guidance and developer pages and did not include any company-specific record pages, officer appointments, filing images, or social media pages for 'ORCHVATE'. Automated API verification was blocked by a missing COMPANIES_HOUSE_API_KEY in the retrieval metadata, preventing programmatic queries that would likely resolve name matches and return filings.

### Overall Confidence

Conclusions that are well-supported: within the supplied material, there is no evidence to verify a UK-registered entity named ORCHVATE, nor any linked officers, filings, dated events, or business contacts (High confidence). Tentative conclusions: the absence of evidence in these specific fetched pages does not prove that ORCHVATE does not exist in Companies House or elsewhere; its absence from the provided pages may reflect an incomplete fetch rather than non-existence (Medium confidence). What would change the assessment: retrieving a Companies House company-detail page, a company number, filing images showing officer appointments/resignations, or corroborating social/business-registry entries that name ORCHVATE would convert the current negative finding into a verified positive. Key caveat: all statements are limited to the supplied materials; no external searches beyond those materials were performed for this report.

[32m+[m[32mdef _source_platform(url: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Classify a URL without making ownership or identity claims."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    parsed = urlparse(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m    host = parsed.netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")[m
[32m+[m[32m    path = parsed.path.lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("linkedin.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "linkedin"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("instagram.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "instagram"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("facebook.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "facebook"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host in {"x.com", "twitter.com", "t.co"} or host.endswith("twitter.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "x"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("youtube.com") or host == "youtu.be":[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "youtube"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if host.endswith("tiktok.com"):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "tiktok"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if "companieshouse.gov.uk" in host or ("gov.uk" in host and "company" in path):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "registry"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if "blog" in path or "blog" in host:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "blog"[m
[32m+[m[32m    return "website"[m
[32m+[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _source_category(platform: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    if platform in {"linkedin", "instagram", "facebook", "x", "youtube", "tiktok"}:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "social"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if platform == "registry":[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "registry"[m
[32m+[m[32m    if platform == "blog":[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "blog"[m
[32m+[m[32m    return "official" if platform == "website" else "web"[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _access_status(content: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Describe observed public access without attempting authentication."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    text = (content or "").lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m    auth_markers = ("agree & join", "sign in", "log in", "login", "create an account")[m
[32m+[m[32m    if any(marker in text for marker in auth_markers):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return "partial_public"[m
[32m+[m[32m    return "fetched"[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _coverage_records([m

[32m+[m[32m                "source_category": _source_category(platform),[m
[32m+[m[32m                "discovery_method": "search_result",[m
[32m+[m[32m                "access_status": _access_status(page.get("content", "")) if page else "not_attempted",[m
[32m+[m[32m                "retrieved_at": page.get("retrieved_at") if page else None,[m
[32m+[m[32m            }[m
[32m+[m[32m        )[m
[32m+[m[32m    for page in fetched_pages:[m
[32m+[m[32m        url = page.get("url", "")[m

[32m+[m[32m            continue[m
[32m+[m[32m        platform = _source_platform(url)[m
[32m+[m[32m        candidates.append([m
[32m+[m[32m            {[m
[32m+[m[32m                "url": url,[m
[32m+[m[32m                "platform": platform,[m
[32m+[m[32m                "source_category": _source_category(platform),[m
[32m+[m[32m                "discovery_method": "official_site_link" if url in official_link_urls else "follow_up_search",[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _enrich_memo_sources(memo_dict: dict, state: ResearchState) -> dict:[m

[32m+[m[32mdef _company_search_term(task: str) -> str:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Extract a compact company token for deterministic discovery queries."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    uppercase = re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Z0-9&.-]{2,}\b", task)[m
[32m+[m[32m    if uppercase:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return uppercase[0][m
[32m+[m[32m    quoted = re.findall(r"[\"']([^\"']{2,80})[\"']", task)[m
[32m+[m[32m    if quoted:[m
[32m+[m[32m        return quoted[0].strip()[m
[32m+[m[32m    return task.strip()[:80][m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _is_company_task(task: str) -> bool:[m
[32m+[m[32m    text = task.lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m    markers = ([m
[32m+[m[32m        "company", "legal entity", "corporate", "business", "organization",[m
[32m+[m[32m        "official website", "social profile", "registry", "company intelligence",[m
[32m+[m[32m    )[m
[32m+[m[32m    return any(marker in text for marker in markers)[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _deterministic_social_queries(task: str, depth: str) -> List[str]:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Return bounded, provider-neutral public discovery queries for company tasks."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    if not _is_company_task(task):[m
[32m+[m[32m        return [][m
[32m+[m[32m    term = _company_search_term(task)[m
[32m+[m[32m    queries = [[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:linkedin.com/company {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:instagram.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:facebook.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:x.com {term} OR site:twitter.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:youtube.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:tiktok.com {term}",[m
[32m+[m[32m        f"site:find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company {term} Companies House",[m
[32m+[m[32m    ][m
[32m+[m[32m    budget = {"brief": 4, "standard": 6, "comprehensive": len(queries)}.get(depth, 6)[m
[32m+[m[32m    return queries[:budget][m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _extract_followable_links(pages: List[dict], max_links: int = 8) -> List[str]:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Extract only same-site or explicitly allowlisted public links from official pages."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    priority_links: List[str] = [][m
[32m+[m[32m    same_site_links: List[str] = [][m
[32m+[m[32m    seen: set[str] = set()[m
[32m+[m[32m    for page in pages:[m
[32m+[m[32m        page_url = page.get("url", "")[m
[32m+[m[32m        page_host = urlparse(page_url).netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")[m
[32m+[m[32m        if not page_host or _source_platform(page_url) in {"linkedin", "instagram", "facebook", "x", "youtube", "tiktok", "registry"}:[m
[32m+[m[32m            continue[m
[32m+[m[32m        for raw in re.findall(r"https?://[^\s<>\]\)\"']+", page.get("content", "")):[m
[32m+[m[32m            candidate = raw.rstrip(".,;:!?")[m
[32m+[m[32m            parsed = urlparse(candidate)[m
[32m+[m[32m            host = parsed.netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")[m
[32m+[m[32m            allowed_external = _source_platform(candidate) in {[m
[32m+[m[32m                "linkedin", "instagram", "facebook", "x", "youtube", "tiktok", "registry"[m
[32m+[m[32m            }[m
[32m+[m[32m            if not parsed.scheme or not host or (host != page_host and not allowed_external):[m
[32m+[m[32m                continue[m
[32m+[m[32m            if candidate not in seen:[m
[32m+[m[32m                seen.add(candidate)[m
[32m+[m[32m                (priority_links if allowed_external else same_site_links).append(candidate)[m
[32m+[m[32m    return (priority_links + same_site_links)[:max_links][m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m
[32m+[m[32mdef _extract_social_terms(pages: List[dict], max_terms: int = 8) -> List[str]:[m
[32m+[m[32m    """Extract public hashtag/mention tokens; matching is not treated as ownership proof."""[m
[32m+[m[32m    terms: List[str] = [][m
[32m+[m[32m    seen: set[str] = set()[m
[32m+[m[32m    for page in pages:[m
[32m+[m[32m        for token in re.findall(r"(?<!\w)[#@][A-Za-z0-9_]{2,50}", page.get("content", "")):[m
[32m+[m[32m            normalized = token.lower()[m
[32m+[m[32m            if normalized not in seen:[m
[32m+[m[32m                seen.add(normalized)[m
[32m+[m[32m                terms.append(token)[m
[32m+[m[32m                if len(terms) >= max_terms:[m
[32m+[m[32m                    return terms[m
[32m+[m[32m    return terms[m

     return get_llm(state["model_name"])[m

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p3_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p3/reports/orchvate_p1_implementation_report.md =====
# Company Intelligence Agent — P1 Implementation Report

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Baseline revision:** `604a79a`

**Phase:** P1 — reliable research and evidence

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE, target jurisdiction United Kingdom

## Scope implemented

The first bounded P1 slice adds provenance and coverage metadata without introducing a database, new external provider, authentication, or platform-access bypass.

### Evidence schema

`src/schemas/research.py` → `ResearchSource` now supports backward-compatible fields for:

- `platform` — website, blog, LinkedIn, Instagram, Facebook, X, YouTube, TikTok, registry, or other.
- `source_category` — official, social, blog, registry, or web.
- `discovery_method` — search result, follow-up search, official-site link, direct registry query, or unknown.
- `access_status` — fetched, partial public, login required, blocked, timeout, not attempted, or unknown.
- `identity_status` — unverified, candidate, corroborated, conflicting, or rejected.
- `publication_date` — only when explicitly available; never inferred.
- `retrieved_at` — UTC retrieval timestamp when the pipeline fetched the page.

All new fields have defaults, so existing LLM responses and historical artifacts remain readable.

### Pipeline coverage

`src/graph/state.py` now includes `source_coverage`.

`src/graph/nodes.py` now:

- Classifies URLs deterministically by host/path.
- Records a UTC retrieval timestamp for successful page fetches.
- Creates coverage records for search-result URLs and follow-up-only pages.
- Labels observed auth-wall markers as `partial_public` without attempting authentication.
- Enriches LLM-selected memo sources with pipeline-observed metadata instead of allowing the model to invent retrieval facts.
- Prints provenance metadata in `researcher_memo.md`.

The implementation does **not** claim that a URL belongs to the target company. `identity_status` remains `unverified` unless later evidence-resolution logic explicitly upgrades it.

## Tests

Command:

```bash
PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/pytest -q
```

Result:

```text
5 passed, 1 warning
```

The warning is an existing third-party `langsmith` deprecation warning (`ast.Str`), not caused by this change.

Additional checks:

- Python compilation passed: `python -m compileall -q src tests`
- `git diff --check` passed.
- No Crawl4AI, Firecrawl, or external repository source was modified.

## ORCHVATE smoke benchmark

Command used a bounded `brief` run with `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY` and `TAVILY_API_KEY` empty, using the existing `openai/gpt-5-mini` model configuration.

Result: **completed successfully**.

Observed output:

- Official website identified: `https://www.orchvate.com/`.
- Public LinkedIn and Facebook profiles were included in the memo.
- Provenance lines were emitted for the website, Facebook, and LinkedIn.
- Facebook and LinkedIn were labeled `partial_public` because retrieved content contained login/guest-access markers.
- Retrieved timestamps were recorded for fetched social pages.
- The report correctly stated that no fetched source verified a UK Companies House entity and that absence of evidence is not proof of non-existence.
- PDF export remained non-fatal and was skipped because `xelatex` is not installed.

The smoke run also demonstrated the current limitation that registry coverage is still generic-search based; official registry connectors are deferred to P3.

## Files changed

- `src/schemas/research.py`
- `src/graph/state.py`
- `src/graph/nodes.py`
- `tests/test_source_provenance.py`

## Files intentionally not changed

- Crawl4AI source or installation.
- Firecrawl source checkout.
- Search-provider credentials or connector configuration.
- Database or persistent company-intelligence schema.
- Authentication, cookies, CAPTCHA handling, or access-control bypass logic.

## Next recommended phase

P1 should continue with typed claims and contradiction records, then P2 can add deterministic social-platform query templates and safe outbound-link discovery. P3 should add an authoritative Companies House connector. PostgreSQL should remain deferred until the structured evidence model and benchmark metrics demonstrate that filesystem/session storage is insufficient.

# Company Intelligence Agent — P2 Implementation Report

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Baseline revision:** `604a79a`

**Phase:** P2 — broad public source discovery

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE, target jurisdiction United Kingdom

## Scope implemented

P2 adds bounded, provider-neutral public-source discovery on top of the P1 provenance layer.

### Deterministic social and registry queries

Company-intelligence tasks now receive deterministic site-restricted discovery queries in addition to the LLM-generated queries:

- LinkedIn company pages
- Instagram
- Facebook
- X/Twitter
- YouTube
- TikTok
- UK Companies House

The query budget is depth-aware:

- `brief`: first four social targets
- `standard`: six social targets
- `comprehensive`: all configured social targets plus the registry query

Non-company comparison or technical tasks do not receive these queries.

### Safe official-site link following

After initial pages are fetched, the pipeline extracts URLs from public page content and follows only:

- same-host links; or
- explicitly allowlisted public social and registry hosts.

External arbitrary links are rejected. Social and registry links are prioritized over ordinary same-site links within a small bounded budget. Links followed from official pages receive `official_site_link` provenance rather than being mislabeled as search results.

No login, cookie, CAPTCHA, or access-control bypass was added.

### Hashtag and mention expansion

Public `#hashtags` and `@mentions` found in retrieved pages are deduplicated and converted into a bounded portion of follow-up search queries. Query execution is recorded in `search_plan`.

A hashtag or mention match remains only a discovery signal; it does not establish ownership or relevance.

## ORCHVATE fresh-cache benchmark

The benchmark used:

- `openai/gpt-5-mini`
- `brief` depth
- Firecrawl and Tavily disabled
- a fresh isolated cache at `/tmp/orchvate-p2-cache`

Result: **completed successfully**.

Observed search plan included:

```text
ORCHVATE UK official website social media ORCHVATE LinkedIn Twitter Instagram Facebook hashtags
ORCHVATE Companies House site:find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk OR site:gov.uk company registration
site:linkedin.com/company ORCHVATE
site:instagram.com ORCHVATE
site:facebook.com ORCHVATE
site:x.com ORCHVATE OR site:twitter.com ORCHVATE
```

Observed direct fetches:

- `https://in.linkedin.com/company/orchvate`
- `https://www.instagram.com/orchvate/`

Observed memo provenance:

- LinkedIn: `platform=linkedin`, `discovery=search_result`, `access=partial_public`, `identity=corroborated`.
- Instagram: `platform=instagram`, `discovery=search_result`, `access=partial_public`, `identity=candidate`.
- UTC retrieval timestamps were recorded for both pages.

The report correctly stated that the run did not verify a UK legal entity and that missing registry evidence is not proof of non-existence.

The benchmark did not happen to select an official same-domain website page among the first fetch budget, so it did not demonstrate an `official_site_link` row in this particular run. The link-following behavior is covered by unit tests and will activate whenever a fetched official page contains eligible links.

PDF export remained non-fatal and was skipped because `xelatex` is not installed.

## Tests and checks

```text
8 tests passed, 1 existing third-party deprecation warning
compileall passed
git diff --check passed
```

## Files changed cumulatively from baseline

- `src/graph/nodes.py`
- `src/graph/state.py`
- `src/schemas/research.py`
- `tests/test_source_provenance.py`

## Safety and limitations

- Public discovery only; no authentication or access-control bypass.
- Search-engine result presence is not identity proof.
- Public social pages can be partial, dynamic, stale, or login-walled.
- Hashtag and mention matches are not ownership proof.
- Companies House remains general-search discovery at P2; authoritative registry verification is deferred to P3.
- No claim is made of complete internet or platform coverage.

## Next recommended phase

P3 should add authoritative registry connectors, typed company/person identity resolution, current-versus-historical role handling, dated events, contradiction records, and public business-contact provenance. PostgreSQL remains deferred until the evidence model and benchmark metrics justify persistent relational storage.

# Company Intelligence Agent — P3 Implementation Report

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Baseline revision:** `604a79a`

**Phase:** P3 — Verification and Company Intelligence

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE, target jurisdiction United Kingdom

## Implemented

P3 adds a provider-independent verification layer without a database or persistent-memory framework.

### Authoritative registry verification

Added `src/verification/company_intelligence.py` with an optional official Companies House API client:

- Uses `COMPANIES_HOUSE_API_KEY` when configured.
- Calls the official Companies House API endpoint with HTTP Basic authentication.
- Produces explicit outcomes:
  - `confirmed_match`
  - `possible_match`
  - `no_match_in_completed_searches`
  - `inconclusive_verification`
- Does not perform a network call when the API key is absent.
- Never converts missing credentials or an incomplete search into a no-match claim.
- Preserves company number, source URL, evidence, retrieval timestamp, confidence, and verification status.

### Company and person identity resolution

Added normalized company-name matching that distinguishes:

- Exact jurisdiction-supported matches.
- Possible name/token overlaps.
- Rejected candidates.

Fetched-page identity candidates remain `candidate` or `unverified`; a social URL, hashtag, or old profile cannot automatically become a confirmed legal identity.

### Current versus historical roles

Added role extraction with status labels:

- `current_claim`
- `historical`

Historical indicators include `former`, `ex-`, `previously`, `left`, and `until`. Role records preserve source URL, evidence, retrieval timestamp, confidence, and verification status.

### Dated events

Added conservative extraction for dated lines associated with event terms such as:

- founded
- launched
- partnered
- acquired
- funding
- joined
- left
- announced
- expanded
- established

Dates are retained only when explicitly present. Publication date is never inferred.

### Contradiction records

Added contradiction detection that groups claims by field and normalized value. Conflicting values are retained together with their source claims and labeled `conflicting`; the system does not choose a winner automatically.

### Business-contact provenance

Added extraction for explicitly published:

- Email addresses
- Phone numbers only when supported by phone/contact context or normal phone formatting
- Contact, booking, WhatsApp, Calendly, and related public channels

The extractor rejects social-platform IDs, navigation URLs, generic registry links, hashtags, and login URLs. Every returned contact is `published_unverified`; no deliverability or ownership is inferred.

### Pipeline and artifacts

Added `verify_company_intelligence_node` between P2 discovery and memo synthesis. P3 records are included in both researcher memo and final report Markdown under **Verification and Company Intelligence**.

## Tests

```text
16 tests passed, 1 existing third-party deprecation warning
compileall passed
git diff --check passed
```

New tests cover:

- Confirmed, possible, and rejected identity matches.
- Registry outcome distinctions.
- No-key Companies House behavior.
- Current versus historical roles.
- Dated event extraction and provenance.
- Contradiction preservation.
- Published-unverified contact provenance.
- Role source provenance.

## Final ORCHVATE benchmark

The fresh-cache P3 pipeline run generated:

- `workdir/orchvate-p3-final2/researcher_memo.md`
- `workdir/orchvate-p3-final2/final_report.md`

Companies House API credentials were intentionally absent. The final artifacts recorded:

```text
Registry outcome: inconclusive_verification
```

The fetched material consisted of generic Companies House/GOV.UK pages rather than an ORCHVATE company record. The final extraction produced no business contacts from those generic pages, avoiding the false attribution seen in the earlier intermediate run. It also avoided treating platform IDs or Facebook navigation links as contacts.

The wrapper command returned exit code 1 only because its final `rg` inspection expected contact lines and found none. The research pipeline itself completed and wrote both artifacts successfully.

## Actual verified facts from this P3 run

- The official Companies House API integration is implemented but was **not executed** because `COMPANIES_HOUSE_API_KEY` was not configured.
- The fetched registry pages were generic registry interfaces/documentation, not an ORCHVATE company record.
- No Companies House legal entity, company number, officer appointment, or registered office was verified in the fetched material.
- No public business contact was accepted as a target-company contact from the final P3 artifact.

## Unresolved limitations

- A register-wide ORCHVATE lookup remains inconclusive without an API key or a completed targeted Companies House search.
- Public search results and social profiles require separate identity corroboration.
- Current employment and directorship status require dated authoritative records; old profiles remain historical or candidate evidence.
- The deterministic extractors are intentionally conservative and may miss nuanced biographies or events.
- No database or persistent memory was introduced, as requested.

# DeepResearchAgent Social-Media Discovery Audit

**Repository:** `/home/ubuntu/DeepRearchAgent`

**Revision audited:** `604a79a`

**Benchmark:** ORCHVATE; target jurisdiction: United Kingdom

**Audit type:** Read-only. No source files were modified.

## Executive summary

The existing repository has a **generic open-web research workflow**, not a social-media discovery subsystem. Its planner asks an LLM for a small list of ordinary web-search queries; its search adapters return generic `{title, url, snippet}` results; and its fetcher sends selected result URLs to Firecrawl or Crawl4AI. There are no platform-specific connectors, platform coverage fields, social-profile entity-resolution rules, registry APIs, cookie/browser-profile handling, or login/access-status schema.

The previous ORCHVATE run demonstrates the following:

- **LinkedIn:** discovered and directly fetched. The company page was accessible only in a public/guest form with sign-in/auth-wall elements. It was corroborated by the company website and had high identity confidence.
- **Facebook:** an official-site footer link was found, but the workflow did not follow or fetch the profile. The URL is evidence of an outbound association claim, not proof that the profile is currently controlled by ORCHVATE.
- **Instagram:** same as Facebook—linked from the official site, not directly fetched or independently verified.
- **X/Twitter:** no company profile URL was found in the existing run artifacts or cached ORCHVATE pages.
- **YouTube:** no ORCHVATE channel URL was found. A page contains the word “YouTuber” in a team/profile context, but that is not evidence of a company channel.
- **TikTok:** no company profile URL was found.
- **Company website/blog:** directly fetched when returned as search results. The site exposes a `/blog.html` link and blog-like pages such as `OurInsights.html` and `SuccessStory.html`, but the fetcher does not automatically crawl all outbound links from a fetched page.
- **Official registries:** the planner generated a Companies House-oriented query, but this was still a general search-engine query. There is no official registry connector or authoritative no-match verification.

## Existing workflow and relevant code paths

### Search planning

- `src/graph/nodes.py:338` — `plan_search_node()` asks the LLM for a fixed number of short web-search queries.
- The prompt requests a general overview, technical-depth, and recent-news/data query. It does **not** require a platform checklist, one query per social network, a registry query, or a source-type coverage matrix.
- `src/graph/nodes.py:459` — `follow_up_searches_node()` asks the LLM to generate gap-filling queries after initial fetching. It can produce a query such as an observed LinkedIn query, but platform coverage is not deterministic or required.

### Search execution

- `src/graph/nodes.py:375` — `execute_searches_node()` runs generated queries and deduplicates URLs.
- `src/tools/web_search.py:3-7,85-100` — search priority is Tavily, then Firecrawl, then DDGS. All providers expose a generic query interface; no platform-specific API or domain allowlist is implemented.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/ddgs_search.py:15-111` — DDGS executes ordinary text search and maps results to generic `SearchItem` objects.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/tavily_search.py:17-101` — Tavily does the same through its general search API.
- `src/tool/default_tools/search/firecrawl_search.py` — Firecrawl search is also generic web search; it is not a social-platform connector.

### Fetching

- `src/graph/nodes.py:429` — `fetch_pages_node()` fetches only URLs already present in search results. It does not extract and enqueue outbound links from fetched pages.
- `src/utils/url_utils.py:95-115` — `fetch_crawl4ai_url()` calls `AsyncWebCrawler().arun(url=url)` with a 15-second timeout.
- `src/utils/url_utils.py:117-169` — `fetch_url()` uses Firecrawl when a key exists, then Crawl4AI, then a PDF fallback. It has no platform-specific status handling.
- `src/tools/web_fetch.py:17-53` — the public fetch tool returns only `url`, `title`, `content`, and `success`.

### State and evidence schemas

- `src/graph/state.py:32-35` — state stores generic `search_plan`, `search_results`, and `fetched_pages`.
- `src/schemas/research.py:35-40` — `ResearchSource` contains only title, URL, and evidence bullets.
- `src/schemas/research.py:43-104` — `ResearchMemo` has source quality notes and open questions but no platform, access status, discovery method, profile-ownership evidence, or registry-record type.
- `src/schemas/research.py:107-180` — `WriterReport` contains generic sections, claims, confidence, and citations but no required social-platform coverage matrix.

### Graph wiring

- `src/graph/graph.py:77-127` wires `detect_mode → plan_search → execute_searches → fetch_pages → follow_up_searches → build_memo → build_report → verify_claims → critique → save_artifacts`.
- There is no `discover_social_profiles`, `extract_outbound_links`, `verify_registry`, or `platform_access_check` node.

## Platform-by-platform findings

Legend:

- **Explicit planner search:** the workflow has a deterministic platform-specific query or connector requirement.
- **General-search discovery:** a generic search may return a platform URL, but the workflow does not require it.
- **Direct crawl:** the URL was passed to the fetcher and content was collected.
- **Access status:** whether public content is likely available without login/cookies/API, based only on observed behavior and implementation—not an attempt to bypass restrictions.

| Platform / source | Explicitly searched by planner? | Could be discovered only through general web search? | Directly crawled in existing ORCHVATE run? | Access / restriction status | ORCHVATE URL and evidence | Confidence that URL belongs to ORCHVATE |

No login/cookies/API were used. Full feed/profile access is restricted. | Profile URL: [LinkedIn company page](https://www.linkedin.com/company/orchvate/). Public X pages can be JavaScript-heavy and can impose login/rate limits; the implementation has no cookies/API/profile support. | No ORCHVATE X/Twitter URL was present in the previous search plan, final source list, or cached ORCHVATE page content reviewed. | **None for a profile**. This is absence of evidence in the run artifacts, not proof that no account exists. |

The cached site text includes “YouTuber” in a team/profile context, but no company-channel URL; that mention must not be promoted to a YouTube profile. | **None for a profile**. |

| **Company website** | **No platform-specific requirement; generic web search can find it.** | **Yes** | **Yes** — official pages were fetched with Crawl4AI. | Public pages were collected successfully. The site links to `/blog.html` and exposes the Facebook, Instagram, and LinkedIn URLs in its footer. | **High** for the official domain as the brand’s public website. |
| **Company blog** | **No** | **Yes** | **Partially.** Blog-like pages were fetched when selected as search results; `/blog.html` was visible as an outbound link but was not automatically enqueued. | Public same-domain content; no login issue observed. Coverage depends on search-result selection. | [Our Insights](https://www.orchvate.com/OurInsights.html), [Success Story](https://www.orchvate.com/SuccessStory.html), and the site’s [blog index](https://www.orchvate.com/blog.html) link. | **High** for same-domain blog association; **partial** for coverage of all posts. |
| **Official company registries** | **No registry connector.** The observed planner included `Orchvate site:gov.uk Companies House`, but this is an ordinary search query. | **Yes** | **No authoritative registry page was fetched.** | No Companies House API/client, registry-specific URL policy, or explicit distinction between “no match,” “search failed,” and “not searched.” No access bypass was attempted. | No authoritative registry URL or registration record was collected. The previous report correctly marked UK legal registration as unverified. | **None for a legal entity/registration number.** |

## Evidence and identity caution

The following evidence levels should not be conflated:

1. **Official-site outbound link:** proves that the ORCHVATE website published a link to a profile URL. It does not prove the profile is active, controlled by the company today, or legally tied to a UK entity.
2. **Profile content:** a fetched profile can provide name, logo, website, address, and descriptions, but those fields may be self-managed and should be checked for consistency.
3. **Independent corroboration:** matching domain, phone, address, staff, or registry data can strengthen identity confidence.
4. **Official registry record:** only a first-party registry record can support a legal-entity name/number conclusion; a search-engine snippet is not sufficient.

The existing `ResearchSource` and `ClaimRecord` structures do not force the agent to record these distinctions. They allow evidence bullets and confidence prose, but not a typed association status such as `linked_from_official_site`, `profile_fetched`, `profile_verified`, or `registry_verified`.

## Access-control assessment

The repository does not attempt to bypass login requirements, CAPTCHA, cookies, or access controls. That is appropriate and should remain the policy.

Observed/implemented behavior:

- **LinkedIn:** Crawl4AI returned guest/public HTML but also exposed sign-in/auth-wall content. The agent must label this as partial public access rather than complete profile access.
- **Instagram/Facebook/X/TikTok/YouTube:** the generic fetcher may attempt public URLs if they appear in search results, but there is no platform-specific handling for JavaScript rendering, rate limits, consent pages, login walls, or API-only fields.
- **No authenticated browser profile:** no code path configures a user’s cookies/session or asks for browser takeover.
- **No official social APIs:** no OAuth clients or platform API keys are defined.
- **No CAPTCHA bypass:** none observed, and none should be added.

## Main gaps

1. **Planner coverage is probabilistic.** Social platforms are not required search targets. A good LLM may generate a LinkedIn query; another run may omit all social networks.
2. **No outbound-link discovery.** The crawler collects page content but does not parse links and enqueue official social/registry URLs.
3. **No platform metadata.** Search and fetch results lose the platform/source type, discovery path, access status, and retrieval outcome.
4. **No profile ownership verification.** The report can cite a URL but cannot systematically compare profile name, website, logo, address, phone, or linked domains to the target company.
5. **No registry integration.** Companies House is searched indirectly through general web search and never verified through a first-party result/API.
6. **No coverage completeness or negative-result semantics.** “Not found,” “not searched,” “search timed out,” “blocked,” and “requires login” are not separate states.
7. **No link-following budget/policy.** The official site exposes Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn, team-member LinkedIn links, and a blog index, but the fetcher does not selectively follow these links.
8. **No social-specific report section.** `WriterReport` does not require a platform matrix, profile URL, evidence link, identity confidence, or access limitation for each platform.
9. **Generic caching can blur benchmark provenance.** Search/page caches are shared across runs, so a strict audit should support a fresh cache or `--no-cache` mode.

## Smallest changes needed for broader public social coverage

These changes can be added without modifying Crawl4AI’s core crawler:

### 1. Require a platform checklist in `plan_search_node`

In `src/graph/nodes.py:338`, extend the planning prompt so company/social tasks produce a bounded set of queries for:

- LinkedIn;
- Instagram;
- Facebook;
- X/Twitter;
- YouTube;
- TikTok;
- official website/blog;
- target-jurisdiction registry.

Keep the number of queries bounded by depth. This is the smallest change, but it remains dependent on LLM compliance.

### 2. Add deterministic query templates

Add a small helper near `plan_search_node` that creates exact site-restricted queries such as:

- `site:linkedin.com/company ORCHVATE`
- `site:instagram.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:facebook.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:x.com ORCHVATE OR site:twitter.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:youtube.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:tiktok.com ORCHVATE`
- `site:gov.uk/companies-house ORCHVATE`

Treat these as discovery queries only, not proof of ownership or registration.

### 3. Extract and enqueue outbound links from fetched official pages

After `fetch_pages_node()` receives a page, parse same-domain and explicitly allowlisted social/registry links. Enqueue only relevant links, with a small budget and deduplication. This would surface the existing Facebook, Instagram, and LinkedIn footer links even when search engines omit them.

Do not blindly crawl every outbound link; use an allowlist for social hosts and jurisdiction-specific registry hosts.

### 4. Extend result/evidence schemas

Add fields to `SearchItem`/`ResearchSource` or a new typed record:

```text
platform: website | blog | linkedin | instagram | facebook | x | youtube | tiktok | registry | other
discovery_method: search | official_site_link | follow_up | direct_registry_query
access_status: fetched | partial_public | login_required | blocked | timeout | not_attempted
identity_status: unverified | possible | corroborated | rejected
identity_evidence: []
```

This is necessary to satisfy the requested audit output reliably.

### 5. Add a social-coverage report section

Require `WriterReport` to include one row per requested platform, even when no account is found. Each row should contain:

- URL or `null`;
- discovery method;
- fetched/not fetched;
- access status;
- evidence linking it to the company;
- confidence;
- explicit statement that absence is not proof of non-existence.

### 6. Add a first-party registry provider separately

For UK due diligence, implement a Companies House provider rather than relying on general search. It must distinguish:

- verified match;
- authoritative no-match;
- query failed;
- blocked/rate-limited;
- insufficient name coverage.

This is essential for legal-entity conclusions and should not be treated as ordinary social discovery.

## Final verdict

The existing DeepResearchAgent can discover public social profiles **opportunistically through generic search** and can directly fetch a public URL returned by search. It does not currently provide reliable, comprehensive social-media discovery.

For ORCHVATE specifically, the existing evidence supports:

- **High confidence:** official website and LinkedIn company-page association.
- **Medium confidence:** Facebook and Instagram URLs are officially linked from the ORCHVATE website, but the profiles were not fetched or independently verified.
- **No profile evidence in this run:** X/Twitter, YouTube, TikTok.
- **Unverified:** UK legal registration, because no first-party registry result was collected.

No source files were modified during this audit; the repository working tree remained clean at revision `604a79a`.

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p3_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p3/source/graph/graph.py =====
"""LangGraph research pipeline assembly.

Compiles the researcher → writer → verifier → critic StateGraph and exposes
`build_research_graph()` for the run script.

Single-researcher path (num_researchers == 1):
  START → detect_mode → plan_search → execute_searches → fetch_pages
        → follow_up_searches → build_memo → build_report → verify_claims
        → critique → [build_report if issues found] → save_artifacts → END

Multi-researcher path (num_researchers > 1):
  START → detect_mode → plan_researchers → execute_parallel_research
        → build_report → verify_claims → critique
        → [build_report if issues found] → save_artifacts → END

verify_claims re-checks low-confidence claims against their cited source
pages (almost always a cache hit), then passes failed verifications to
critique as hard evidence of unsupported claims.

Human-in-the-loop (interactive=True):
  Execution pauses after plan_search (single-researcher) or
  plan_researchers (multi-researcher) for user review / query editing.
  Requires a LangGraph checkpointer (MemorySaver injected automatically).

LangSmith traces every node automatically when
LANGCHAIN_TRACING_V2=true is set in the environment.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph

from src.graph.nodes import (
    build_memo_node,
    build_report_node,
    critique_node,
    detect_mode_node,
    execute_parallel_research_node,
    execute_searches_node,
    fetch_pages_node,
    follow_up_searches_node,
    plan_researchers_node,
    plan_search_node,
    save_artifacts_node,
    verify_company_intelligence_node,
    verify_claims_node,
)
from src.graph.state import ResearchState


def _route_after_detect_mode(state: ResearchState) -> str:
    if state.get("num_researchers", 1) > 1:
        return "plan_researchers"
    return "plan_search"


def _route_after_critique(state: ResearchState) -> str:
    """Revise once if the critic found significant issues; otherwise proceed."""
    critique = state.get("critique_result")
    revision_count = state.get("revision_count", 0)
    if critique and critique.get("has_issues") and revision_count < 2:
        return "build_report"
    return "save_artifacts"


def build_research_graph(interactive: bool = False):
    """Build and compile the deep-research LangGraph pipeline.

    Args:
        interactive: When True, the graph pauses after plan_search (single-researcher)
            and plan_researchers (multi-researcher) to allow human review/editing of the
            query plan or sub-topic list before research begins. Requires calling
            ainvoke() twice — once to reach the checkpoint, once to resume.
            A MemorySaver checkpointer is injected automatically.
    """
    workflow = StateGraph(ResearchState)

    # Register all nodes
    workflow.add_node("detect_mode", detect_mode_node)
    workflow.add_node("plan_search", plan_search_node)
    workflow.add_node("execute_searches", execute_searches_node)
    workflow.add_node("fetch_pages", fetch_pages_node)
    workflow.add_node("follow_up_searches", follow_up_searches_node)
    workflow.add_node("verify_company_intelligence", verify_company_intelligence_node)
    workflow.add_node("build_memo", build_memo_node)
    workflow.add_node("plan_researchers", plan_researchers_node)
    workflow.add_node("execute_parallel_research", execute_parallel_research_node)
    workflow.add_node("build_report", build_report_node)
    workflow.add_node("verify_claims", verify_claims_node)
    workflow.add_node("critique", critique_node)
    workflow.add_node("save_artifacts", save_artifacts_node)

    # Entry point
    workflow.add_edge(START, "detect_mode")

    # Branch: single-researcher vs multi-researcher
    workflow.add_conditional_edges(
        "detect_mode",
        _route_after_detect_mode,
        {
            "plan_researchers": "plan_researchers",
            "plan_search": "plan_search",
        },
    )

    # Single-researcher path
    workflow.add_edge("plan_search", "execute_searches")
    workflow.add_edge("execute_searches", "fetch_pages")
    workflow.add_edge("fetch_pages", "follow_up_searches")
    workflow.add_edge("follow_up_searches", "verify_company_intelligence")
    workflow.add_edge("verify_company_intelligence", "build_memo")
    workflow.add_edge("build_memo", "build_report")

    # Multi-researcher path
    workflow.add_edge("plan_researchers", "execute_parallel_research")
    workflow.add_edge("execute_parallel_research", "build_report")

    # Common tail: report → verify_claims → critique → (revise or save)
    workflow.add_edge("build_report", "verify_claims")
    workflow.add_edge("verify_claims", "critique")
    workflow.add_conditional_edges(
        "critique",
        _route_after_critique,
        {
            "build_report": "build_report",
            "save_artifacts": "save_artifacts",
        },
    )

    workflow.add_edge("save_artifacts", END)

    if interactive:
        from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver
        return workflow.compile(
            checkpointer=MemorySaver(),
            interrupt_after=["plan_search", "plan_researchers"],
        )

    return workflow.compile()

===== FILE: orchvate_company_intelligence_p3_bundle/orchvate_company_intelligence_p3/source/graph/nodes.py =====
"""LangGraph node implementations for the deep-research pipeline.

Each node is an async function that receives the full ResearchState and
returns a dict of partial state updates.  LangGraph merges the updates
before calling the next node.

Single-researcher pipeline:
  detect_mode → plan_search → execute_searches → fetch_pages
              → follow_up_searches → build_memo
              → build_report → critique → [build_report] → save_artifacts

Multi-researcher pipeline (num_researchers > 1):
  detect_mode → plan_researchers → execute_parallel_research
              → build_report → critique → [build_report] → save_artifacts

Depth modes control query count, pages fetched, and follow-up searches:
  brief        — 2 queries, 3 pages, no follow-up
  standard     — 3 queries, 4 pages, 2 follow-up queries
  comprehensive — 5 queries, 6 pages, 3 follow-up queries
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import json
import os
import re
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple, Type, TypeVar
from urllib.parse import urlparse

from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, SystemMessage
from pydantic import BaseModel

from src.graph.state import ResearchState
from src.llm.factory import get_llm
from src.memory import DataType, MemoryManager, get_manager
from src.memory.manager import search_cache_key
from src.schemas.research import (
    ClaimRecord,
    ClaimVerificationResult,
    CritiqueResult,
    ReportSection,
    ResearchMemo,
    SourceVerdict,
    TaskAnalysis,
    WriterReport,
)
from src.tools.web_fetch import WebFetchTool
from src.tools.web_search import WebSearchTool
from src.verification.company_intelligence import (
    detect_contradictions,
    extract_business_contacts,
    extract_dated_events,
    extract_identity_candidates,
    extract_roles,
    search_companies_house,
)

# Module-level singletons — shared across all graph invocations
_web_search = WebSearchTool()
_web_fetch = WebFetchTool()

SchemaT = TypeVar("SchemaT", bound=BaseModel)


def _get_memory(state: ResearchState) -> Optional[MemoryManager]:
    """Return the MemoryManager for this session, or None if no session_id."""
    session_id = state.get("session_id")
    if not session_id:
        return None
    return get_manager(session_id, state.get("workdir", ""))


async def _fetch_page(url: str) -> Optional[dict]:
    """Fetch one URL and return {url, title, content}, or None on failure.

    Single shared implementation used by all fetch sites so the cache
    integration and timeout are consistent everywhere.
    """
    try:
        result = await asyncio.wait_for(_web_fetch.ainvoke({"url": url}), timeout=20.0)
        if isinstance(result, dict) and result.get("success") and result.get("content"):
            return {
                "url": result.get("url", url),
                "title": result.get("title", "Untitled Source"),
                "content": result.get("content", ""),
                "retrieved_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
            }
    except (asyncio.TimeoutError, Exception):
        pass
    return None

def _source_platform(url: str) -> str:
    """Classify a URL without making ownership or identity claims."""
    parsed = urlparse(url)
    host = parsed.netloc.lower().split(":", 1)[0].removeprefix("www.")
    path = parsed.path.lower()
    if host.endswith("linkedin.com"):
        return "linkedin"
    if host.endswith("instagram.com"):
        return "instagram"
    if host.endswith("facebook.com"):
        return "facebook"
    if host in {"x.com", "twitter.com", "t.co"} or host.endswith("twitter.com"):
        return "x"
    if host.endswith("youtube.com") or host == "youtu.be":
        return "youtube"
    if host.endswith("tiktok.com"):
        return "tiktok"
    if "companieshouse.gov.uk" in host or ("gov.uk" in host and "company" in path):
        return "registry"
    if "blog" in path or "blog" in host:
        return "blog"
    return "website"


def _source_category(platform: str) -> str:
    if platform in {"linkedin", "instagram", "facebook", "x", "youtube", "tiktok"}:
        return "social"
    if platform == "registry":
        return "registry"
    if platform == "blog":
        return "blog"
    return "official" if platform == "website" else "web"


def _access_status(content: str) -> str:
    """Describe observed public access without attempting authentication."""
    text = (content or "").lower()
    auth_markers = ("agree & join", "sign in", "log in", "login", "create an account")
    if any(marker in text for marker in auth_markers):
        return "partial_public"
    return "fetched"


def _coverage_records(
    search_results: List[dict],
    fetched_pages: List[dict],
    official_link_urls: Optional[set[str]] = None,
) -> List[dict]:
    """Create auditable candidate-URL coverage records from observed pipeline data."""
    official_link_urls = official_link_urls or set()
    fetched_by_url = {p.get("url"): p for p in fetched_pages if p.get("url")}
    candidates: List[dict] = []
    seen: set[str] = set()
    for item in search_results:
        url = item.get("url", "")
        if not url or url in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(url)
        page = fetched_by_url.get(url)
        platform = _source_platform(url)
        candidates.append(
            {
                "url": url,
                "platform": platform,
                "source_category": _source_category(platform),
                "discovery_method": "search_result",
                "access_status": _access_status(page.get("content", "")) if page else "not_attempted",
                "retrieved_at": page.get("retrieved_at") if page else None,
            }
        )
    for page in fetched_pages:
        url = page.get("url", "")
        if not url or url in seen:
            continue
        platform = _source_platform(url)
        candidates.append(
            {
                "url": url,
                "platform": platform,
                "source_category": _source_category(platform),
                "discovery_method": "official_site_link" if url in official_link_urls else "follow_up_search",
                "access_status": _access_status(page.get("content", "")),
                "retrieved_at": page.get("retrieved_at"),
            }
        )
    return candidates


def _enrich_memo_sources(memo_dict: dict, state: ResearchState) -> dict:
    """Attach pipeline-observed provenance to LLM-selected sources; never infer ownership."""
    coverage = {item["url"]: item for item in state.get("source_coverage", []) if item.get("url")}
    fetched = {item.get("url"): item for item in state.get("fetched_pages", []) if item.get("url")}
    for source in memo_dict.get("sources", []):
        url = source.get("url", "")
        observed = coverage.get(url)
        if observed:
            source.update({k: v for k, v in observed.items() if v is not None})
        else:
            platform = _source_platform(url)
            source.update(
                {
                    "platform": platform,
                    "source_category": _source_category(platform),
                    "access_status": _access_status(fetched[url].get("content", "")) if url in fetched else "not_attempted",
                    "discovery_method": "unknown",
                }
            )
        source.setdefault("identity_status", "unverified")
        if url in fetched and fetched[url].get("retrieved_at"):
            source["retrieved_at"] = fetched[url]["retrieved_at"]
    return memo_dict


def _company_search_term(task: str) -> str:
    """Extract a compact company token for deterministic discovery queries."""
    uppercase = re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Z0-9&.-]{2,}\b", task)
    if uppercase:
        return uppercase[0]
    quoted = re.findall(r"[\"']([^\"']{2,80})[\"']", task)
    if quoted:
        return quoted[0].strip()
    return task.strip()[:80]


def _is_company_task(task: str) -> bool:
    text = task.lower()
    markers = (
        "company", "legal entity", "corporate", "business", "organization",
        "official website", "social profile", "registry", "company intelligence",
    )
